\displaystyle\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})

\displaystyle:=(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\times\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}))\times S^{d-1}

\displaystyle~=(S^{d-1})^{2^{k+1}-1},

—

the group

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}

\displaystyle:=(\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}

\displaystyle~=\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\wr\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}\wr\cdots\wr\mathbb{Z}_{2}\qquad(k+1\text{ times}),

—

the action of the group
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}
on
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
by

\displaystyle(h_{1},h_{2})\cdot(y_{1},y_{2},x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(h_{1}\cdot y_{1},h_{2}\cdot y_{2},x),

\displaystyle(h_{1},h_{2},\omega)\cdot(y_{1},y_{2},x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(h_{2}\cdot y_{2},h_{1}\cdot y_{1},-x),

where
(h_{1},h_{2})\in\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\subseteq(\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
,
\omega
is the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\subseteq(\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, and
(y_{1},y_{2},x)\in(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\times\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}))\times S^{d-1}
,

—

the Ptolemaic epicycles embedding

\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}\colon\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})\longrightarrow\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})

by

\qquad\qquad\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}(y_{1},y_{2},x):=(x+\varepsilon\,\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{1}(y_{1}),\ldots,x+\varepsilon\,\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{2^{k}}(y_{1}),\\
-x+\varepsilon\,\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{1}(y_{2}),\ldots,-x+\varepsilon\,\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{2^{k}}(y_{2})),

—

the embedding
\iota_{k+1}\colon\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{2^{k+1}}
that is defined by

\displaystyle\iota_{k+1}(h_{1},h_{2})(i)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\iota_{k}(h_{1})(i),&1\leq i\leq 2^{k},\\
\iota_{k}(h_{2})(i-2^{k})+2^{k},&2^{k}+1\leq i\leq 2^{k+1},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\iota_{k+1}(\omega)(i)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}i+2^{k},&1\leq i\leq 2^{k},\\
i-2^{k},&2^{k}+1\leq i\leq 2^{k+1}.\end{cases}

This, in particular, means that the subgroup
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times 1
permutes elements of
[2^{k+1}]_{1}
while keeping elements of
[2^{k+1}]_{2}
fixed, the subgroup
1\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}
on the other hand permutes elements of
[2^{k+1}]_{2}
and fixes elements of
[n]_{1}
.
The subgroup generated by
\omega
interchanges the blocks
[2^{k+1}]_{1}
and
[2^{k+1}]_{2}
. In addition
\iota_{k+1}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}})
is a Sylow
2
-subgroup of
\mathfrak{S}_{2^{k+1}}
.
For an illustration of the embedding
\operatorname{ecy}_{2,2}\colon\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{2},2)\longrightarrow\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{2},2)
see Figure
5
.

Then
\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}
is an
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}
-equivariant map if the action of
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}
on
\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
is given by

\displaystyle(h_{1},h_{2})\cdot(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2^{k+1}})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(h_{1}\cdot(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2^{k}}),h_{2}\cdot(z_{2^{k}+1},\ldots,z_{2^{k+1}})),

\displaystyle(h_{1},h_{2},\omega)\cdot(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2^{k+1}})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(h_{2}\cdot(z_{2^{k}+1},\ldots,z_{2^{k+1}}),h_{1}\cdot(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2^{k}})),

for
(h_{1},h_{2})\in\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}
,
\omega
the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, and
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2^{k+1}})\in\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
.
In other words, the action of
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}
on
\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
is given via the embedding
\iota_{k+1}
.

Figure 5.
An illustration of the embedding
\operatorname{ecy}_{2,2}\colon\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{2},2)\longrightarrow\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{2},2)
.

Remark 2.2
.

An analogous construction can be given using the little cubes operad
\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)
in the place of the sphere
S^{d-1}
.
(For more details on little cubes operad see for example
[
76
]
or consult Section
7.1.3
.)
Indeed, let
d\geq 2
be an integer or
d=\infty
, and let
m\geq 0
be also an integer.
We define the space
\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
of
little cubes epicycles space
and the corresponding
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
-eqivariant map
\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2^{m}}\colon\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m})
as follows.

(1)

If
m=0
, then we set
\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1):=\mathcal{C}_{d}(1)
, and
\operatorname{cecy}_{d,1}\colon\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{d}(1)
is the identity map.
The group
\mathcal{S}_{1}=1
acts on both spaces
\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)
and
\mathcal{C}_{d}(1)
trivially, and so
\operatorname{cecy}_{d,1}
is an
\mathcal{S}_{1}
-equivariant map.

(2)

If
m=1
, then we set
\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2):=(\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)\times\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)
, and the map

\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2}\colon\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)

is the composition map

\textstyle{(\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)\times\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\operatorname{cecy}_{d,1}\times\operatorname{cecy}_{d,1})\times\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\ (\mathcal{C}_{d}(1)\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(1))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{d}(2),}

where
\mu
denotes the structural map of the little cubes operad, as defined in Section
7.1.3
.
The group
\mathcal{S}_{2}=(\mathcal{S}_{1}\times\mathcal{S}_{1})\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{2}\lhook\joinrel\xrightarrow{~\iota_{1}~}\mathfrak{S}_{2}
coincides with the group
\mathcal{S}_{1,1;2}
defined in Lemma
7.2
and acts on
\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)
as follows:

\displaystyle(h_{1},h_{2})\cdot(y_{1},y_{2},x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(h_{1}\cdot y_{1},h_{2}\cdot y_{2},x),

\displaystyle(h_{1},h_{2},\omega)\cdot(y_{1},y_{2},x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(h_{2}\cdot y_{2},h_{1}\cdot y_{1},-x),

where
(h_{1},h_{2})\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\times\mathcal{S}_{1}\subseteq(\mathcal{S}_{1}\times\mathcal{S}_{1})\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{2}
,
\omega
is the generator of
\mathfrak{S}_{2}\subseteq(\mathcal{S}_{1}\times\mathcal{S}_{1})\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{2}
, and
(y_{1},y_{2},x)\in(\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)\times\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)
.
The assumed action of
\mathcal{S}_{2}=\mathcal{S}_{1,1;2}
on
(\mathcal{C}_{d}(1)\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(1))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)
is described in Section
7.1.3
.
Finally the action of
\mathcal{S}_{2}
on
\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)
is given via embedding
\iota_{1}\colon\mathcal{S}_{2}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{2}
.
With these actions both maps
(\operatorname{cecy}_{d,1}\times\operatorname{cecy}_{d,1})\times\operatorname{id}
and
\mu
are
\mathcal{S}_{2}
-equivariant, and consequently the composition map
\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2}
is a
\mathcal{S}_{2}
-equivariant.
It is important to notice that
\mathcal{S}_{1}=1
and so
\mathcal{S}_{2}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}\cong\mathfrak{S}_{2}
.

(3)

Let us assume that for
m=k
we have defined the space

\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})=(\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k-1})\times\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k-1}))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2),

the embedding
\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2^{k}}\colon\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{2}(2^{k})
, the embedding
\iota_{k}
of the Sylow
2
-subgroup
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}
into
\mathfrak{S}_{2^{k}}
, and the action of the group
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}
on
\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})
in such a way that
\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2^{k}}
is a
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}
-equivariant map.

For
m=k+1
we define the space

\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1}):=(\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\times\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2).

The action of the group
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}
on
\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
is given by

\displaystyle(h_{1},h_{2})\cdot(y_{1},y_{2},x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(h_{1}\cdot y_{1},h_{2}\cdot y_{2},x),

\displaystyle(h_{1},h_{2},\omega)\cdot(y_{1},y_{2},x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(h_{2}\cdot y_{2},h_{1}\cdot y_{1},-x),

where
(h_{1},h_{2})\in\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\subseteq(\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}})\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{2}
,
\omega
is the generator of
\mathfrak{S}_{2}\subseteq(\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}})\rtimes\mathfrak{S}_{2}
, and
(y_{1},y_{2},x)\in(\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\times\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)
.
The map

\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}\colon\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{k+1})

is defined to be the following composition

\textstyle{(\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\times\operatorname{Ce}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2^{k}}\times\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2^{k}})\times\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}}
\textstyle{\ (\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{k})\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{k}))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{k+1}),}

where
\mu
denotes the structural map of the little cubes operad.
Under assumed actions, by direct inspection, we get that the two maps

(\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2^{k}}\times\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2^{k}})\times\operatorname{id}
and
\mu

are both
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}
-equivariant.
Consequently the composition map
\operatorname{cecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}
is also
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}
-equivariant.

Example 2.3
.

Let
m=2
.
Then the bijection
\beta\colon\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\oplus 2}\longrightarrow[4]
is given by

(0,0)\longmapsto 1,\quad(0,1)\longmapsto 2,\quad(1,0)\longmapsto 3,\quad(1,1)\longmapsto 4.

The group
\mathcal{S}_{2}=(\mathcal{S}_{1}\times\mathcal{S}_{1})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\langle\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\rangle\rtimes\langle\omega\rangle\cong(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}
embeds via
\iota_{2}
into the symmetric group
\mathfrak{S}_{4}
by sending generators to the following permutations

\varepsilon_{1}\longmapsto{1234\choose 2134},\qquad\varepsilon_{2}\longmapsto{1234\choose 1243},\qquad\omega\longmapsto{1234\choose 3412}.

Let
n\geq 1
be an integer.
We consider the following vector subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
:

W_{n}:=\{(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:a_{1}+\cdots+a_{n}=0\}.

Then the subspace
\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{n}:x_{1}+\cdots+x_{n}=0\}
of
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{n}
can be identified with the direct sum
W_{n}^{\oplus d}
.
The map
\operatorname{ecy}_{d,n}
that we have defined has the following property.

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
d\geq 2
be an integer or
d=\infty
, and let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
Then
\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}})\subseteq W_{2^{m}}^{\oplus d}
, that is, for every
(y_{1},y_{2},x)\in\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})

\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}^{1}(y_{1},y_{2},x)+\cdots+\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}^{2^{m}}(y_{1},y_{2},x)=0.

Proof.

We use induction on the integer
m\geq 0
.
For
m=0
we have that

\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{ecy}_{d,1})=\{0\}=W_{1}^{\oplus d}.

Assume that for
m=k>0
we have
\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}})\subseteq W_{2^{k}}^{\oplus d}
.
Then for
m=k+1
and
(y_{1},y_{2},x)\in\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})=(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\times\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}))\times S^{d-1}
, using the induction hypothesis, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2^{k+1}}\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}^{j}(y_{1},y_{2},x)

\displaystyle=(x+\varepsilon\,\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{1}(y_{1}))+\cdots+(x+\varepsilon\,\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{2^{k}}(y_{1}))+

\displaystyle\quad\ (-x+\varepsilon\,\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{1}(y_{2}))+\cdots+(-x+\varepsilon\,\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{2^{k}}(y_{2}))

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\big(\sum_{j=1}^{2^{k}}\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{j}(y_{1})\big)+\varepsilon\big(\sum_{j=1}^{2^{k}}\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{j}(y_{2})\big)

\displaystyle=0.

Consequently
\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k+1}})\subseteq W_{2^{k+1}}^{\oplus d}
, and the induction is completed.
∎

Figure 6.
An illustration of the embedding
\operatorname{ecy}_{2,4}\colon\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{2},4)\longrightarrow\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{2},4)
.

Next we verify that the Ptolemaic epicycles embedding
\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}
is indeed an embedding, see
[
64
, Lem. 1.6]
.

Proposition 2.5
.

Let
d\geq 2
be an integer or
d=\infty
, and let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
For any fixed real number
0<\varepsilon<\tfrac{1}{3}
the map

\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}\colon\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})\longrightarrow\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})

is an embedding.

Proof.

The continuity of the map
\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}
follows directly from Definition
2.1
.
Thus, we only need to show that
\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}
is an injective map.
For that we use induction on integer
m\geq 0
.

The map
\operatorname{ecy}_{d,1}\colon\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)\longrightarrow\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)
is evidently injective, since
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)=\mathrm{pt}
.
Assume that for
m=k\geq 0
the map
\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}
is injective.
Now, for
m=k+1
, suppose that

\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}(y_{1},y_{2},x)=\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime},x^{\prime}),

(10)

where
(y_{1},y_{2},x),(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime},x^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
.
Consequently, the sums of first
2^{k}
coordinates must coincide

\sum_{j=1}^{2^{k}}\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}^{j}(y_{1},y_{2},x)=\sum_{j=1}^{2^{k}}\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}^{j}(y_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime},x^{\prime}).

From the definition of the map
\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k+1}}
it follows that

2^{k}x+\varepsilon\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{2^{k}}\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{j}(y_{1})\Big)=2^{k}x^{\prime}+\varepsilon\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{2^{k}}\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{j}(y_{1}^{\prime})\Big).

From Proposition
2.4
we get

\sum_{j=1}^{2^{k}}\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{j}(y_{1})=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sum_{j=1}^{2^{k}}\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}^{j}(y_{1}^{\prime})=0,

implying that
x=x^{\prime}
.
Furthermore, using Definition
2.1
and (
10
) we have that

\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}(y_{1})=\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}(y_{1}^{\prime})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}(y_{2})=\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{k}}(y_{2}^{\prime}).

Finally the induction hypothesis implies that
y_{1}=y_{1}^{\prime}
and
y_{2}=y_{2}^{\prime}
concluding the proof of the proposition.
∎

In the case when
d=\infty
the space
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
is a contractible space with a free
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
-action, and therefore is a model for
\mathrm{E}\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
.
In particular, we can observe that

\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})\cong\operatorname{colim}_{d\to\infty}\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m}),

where the colimit is defined via the inclusions
\mathbb{R}^{d}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
,
x\longmapsto(x,0)
, which induce the corresponding inclusion maps
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d+1},2^{m})
.
Furthermore, the induced
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
-equivariant map
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})\longrightarrow\operatorname{colim}_{d\to\infty}\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
is given by the inclusion maps
\mathbb{R}^{d}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\infty}
,
x\longmapsto(x,0,0,\ldots)
, and is denoted by

\kappa_{d,2^{m}}\colon\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m}).

(11)

In summary, we have the following.

Proposition 2.6
.

Let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
Then
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})
is a free contractible
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
-CW complex.

Before we continue towards the study of the equivariant cohomology of the space
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
, we specify an elementary abelian subgroup
\mathcal{E}_{m}
of the Sylow
2
-subgroup
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
of the symmetric group
\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}
.

Definition 2.7
.

Let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
The subgroup
\mathcal{E}_{m}
of the group
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
is defined inductively as follows.

(1)

If
m=0
, then we set
\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\mathcal{S}_{1}=1
.

(2)

If
m=1
, then we set
\mathcal{E}_{1}:=\mathcal{S}_{2}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

(3)

Let us assume that for
m=k\geq 1
we have defined the subgroup
\mathcal{E}_{k}
of
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}
.

(4)

If
m=k+1
and
\delta\colon\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}
denotes the diagonal monomorphism given by
s\mapsto(s,s)
, then we set

\mathcal{E}_{k+1}:=\delta(\mathcal{E}_{k})\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\cong\mathcal{E}_{k}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\oplus k+1}\subseteq(\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}.

Furthermore, let
C_{1},\ldots,C_{m}
be cyclic groups isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
with the property that

\mathcal{E}_{i}=(C_{1}\times\cdots\times C_{i-1})\times C_{i}=\delta(\mathcal{E}_{i-1})\times C_{i}.

Here we make slight abuse of notation identifying
\mathcal{E}_{i-1}
with a subgroup of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
.
Having this decomposition fixed we see that
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}=C_{1}\wr\cdots\wr C_{m}
.
In summary, we have inclusions of the groups

\mathcal{E}_{m}\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}

(12)

where
\mathcal{E}_{m}
is the subgroup of
\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}
given by translations (seen as permutations) of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\oplus m}
, the so called regular embedded subgroup
[
2
, Ex. III.2.7]
, and the inclusion
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}
is defined via the monomorphism
\iota_{m}
.

The cohomology of the elementary abelian group
\mathcal{E}_{m}
with
\mathbb{F}_{2}
coefficients is well known.
In particular, we fix the following presentation

H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})=\mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}],

where
\deg(y_{k})=1
for
1\leq k\leq m
, in such a way that the exact sequence of groups

\textstyle{1\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{E}_{m-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{E}_{m-1}\times C_{m}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ C_{m}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ 1}

induces the following sequence of algebras

\textstyle{0\ }
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{m-1}]\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{m-1},y_{m}]\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{m}]\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ 0\ ,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

which is exact in each positive degree.

3.
The equivariant cohomology of
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})

Let
d\geq 2
be an integer or
d=\infty
, and let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
In this section we study the equivariant cohomology of the space
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
with respect to the already defined free action of the group
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
, that is,

H^{*}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m});\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\\
\cong H^{*}(\mathrm{E}\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\times_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m});\mathbb{F}_{2}).

In particular, if
d=\infty
, then according to Proposition
2.6
we have that

H^{*}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m});\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

Since the space
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
is defined inductively, our computation utilizes this feature, and is an alternative to the calculation presented in
[
64
, Sec. 2]
.
The methods we use are applicable in a more general setting.

3.1.
Small values of
m

Let us first consider the case when
m=0
.
In this case, according to Definition
2.1
, we have that

\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)=\mathrm{pt}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{S}_{1}=1.

Consequently

H^{r}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1);\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H^{r}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)/\mathcal{S}_{1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H^{r}(\mathrm{pt};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\begin{cases}\mathbb{F}_{2},&r=0,\\
0,&r\neq 0.\end{cases}

We specify a particular additive basis for the cohomology
H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)/\mathcal{S}_{1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
by

\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1):=\{1\},

where
1
is the generator of
H^{0}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)/\mathcal{S}_{1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}
.
In addition we partition the set
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)
into two subsets

\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1):=\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{B}_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1):=\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1){\setminus}\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1).

Thus,
\mathcal{B}_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1)=\varnothing
.

Next, let
m=1
.
Then from Definition
2.1
we get

\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)=S^{d-1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{S}_{2}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

Therefore

\displaystyle H^{r}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2);\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\cong H^{r}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)/\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\cong\begin{cases}\mathbb{F}_{2},&0\leq r\leq d-1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Again we specify an additive basis for the cohomology
H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)/\mathcal{S}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})
as follows:

\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2):=\{1,e,e^{2},\ldots,e^{d-1}\},

where
e^{i}
is the generator of
H^{i}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}
.
The partition of the basis
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)
we use is defined by:

\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2):=\{(x\otimes x)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}e^{i}:x\in\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},1),\,0\leq i\leq d-1\}=\{1,e,e^{2},\ldots,e^{d-1}\},

and

\mathcal{B}_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2):=\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2){\setminus}\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)=\varnothing.

3.2.
The case
m=2

The first interesting case is when
m=2
.
Again from Definition
2.1
we have that

\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})=\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)=(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)\times\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2))\times S^{d-1}=(S^{d-1}\times S^{d-1})\times S^{d-1},

and

\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}=\mathcal{S}_{4}=(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}\wr\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\big(\langle\varepsilon_{1}\rangle\times\langle\varepsilon_{2}\rangle\big)\rtimes\langle\omega\rangle.

(The group
\mathcal{S}_{4}
is isomorphic to the dihedral group
D_{8}
.)
The free action of the group
\mathcal{S}_{4}
on
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)
we introduced is given by

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{1}\cdot(y_{1},y_{2},x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-y_{1},y_{2},x),

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{2}\cdot(y_{1},y_{2},x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(y_{1},-y_{2},x),

\displaystyle\omega\cdot(y_{1},y_{2},x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(y_{2},y_{1},-x),

for
(y_{1},y_{2},x)\in S^{d-1}\times(S^{d-1}\times S^{d-1})
.
Thus

\displaystyle\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)/\mathcal{S}_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big((S^{d-1}\times S^{d-1})\times S^{d-1}\big)/\mathcal{S}_{4}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\big((S^{d-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times S^{d-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times S^{d-1}\big)/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\big((\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\times S^{d-1}\big)/\mathbb{Z}_{2}=:(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}.

The action of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
on
(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\times S^{d-1}
we have is given by

(u_{1},u_{2},x)\longmapsto(u_{2},u_{1},-x)

for
(u_{1},u_{2},x)\in(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\times S^{d-1}
.
Since this
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action is free then the corresponding quotient space
(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}
is the total space of the fiber bundle

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1},}

(13)

where the map
p
is induced by the (
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant) projection on the second factor

(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\times S^{d-1}\longrightarrow S^{d-1}.

Furthermore, the
\mathcal{S}_{4}
-equivariant inclusion
\kappa_{d,4}\colon\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},4)
, introduced in (
11
), induces the following morphism of fiber bundles

\textstyle{(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\kappa_{d,4}/\mathcal{S}_{4}}
\textstyle{\ (\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}.}

(14)

For every integer
d\geq 2
or
d=\infty
the fiber bundle (
13
) induces a Serre spectral sequence that converges to
H^{*}((\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
The
E_{2}
-term of this spectral sequence is of the form

E_{2}^{r,s}(d)=H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})).

(15)

Here
\mathcal{H}^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
denotes a local coefficient system determined by the action of the fundamental group of the base
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})
on the cohomology of the fiber
H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
The cohomology ring of the fiber, via the Künneth formula
[
21
, Thm. VI.3.2]
, can be presented in the following way

\displaystyle H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\cong H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\otimes H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[e_{1}]/\langle e_{1}^{d}\rangle\otimes\mathbb{F}_{2}[e_{2}]/\langle e_{2}^{d}\rangle

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[e_{1},e_{2}]/\langle e_{1}^{d},e_{2}^{d}\rangle,

where
\deg(e_{1})=\deg(e_{2})=1
.
Here
\langle e_{1}^{d},e_{2}^{d}\rangle
denotes the ideal in
\mathbb{F}_{2}[e_{1},e_{2}]
generated by the polynomials
e_{1}^{d}
and
e_{2}^{d}
.
The fundamental group
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})=\langle t\rangle
is a cyclic group.
Indeed,
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1})\cong\mathbb{Z}
and
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
for
d\geq 3
.
The action of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})
on the cohomology ring
H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
is given by
t\cdot e_{1}=e_{2}
.

In the case when
d=\infty
the spectral sequence (
15
) becomes

E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)=H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathbb{F}_{2}))\cong H^{r}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};H^{s}(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})).

(16)

Now, the cohomology ring of the fiber, via the Künneth formula
[
21
, Thm. VI.3.2]
, can be presented in the following way

\displaystyle H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\cong H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathbb{F}_{2})\otimes H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[e_{1}]\otimes\mathbb{F}_{2}[e_{2}]\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[e_{1},e_{2}],

where
\deg(e_{1})=\deg(e_{2})=1
.
Notice an abuse of notation occurring in naming of the generators of the cohomology of the fibers in spectral sequences (
15
) and (
16
).
The fundamental group
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty})=\langle t\rangle\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
acts on the cohomology ring of the fiber
H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathbb{F}_{2})
by
t\cdot e_{1}=e_{2}
.

Figure 7.
The morphism
E_{2}^{r,s}(\kappa_{d,n})
.

Next, we consider the morphism between the spectral sequences (
15
) and (
16
) induced by the morphism of fiber bundles (
14
) on the level of
E_{2}
-terms:

\textstyle{E_{2}^{r,s}(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{E_{2}^{r,s}(\kappa_{d,4}/\mathcal{S}_{4})}
\textstyle{H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2}))\ }
\textstyle{\ H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathbb{F}_{2}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{E_{2}^{r,s}(\kappa_{d,4}/\mathcal{S}_{4})}

Then we have that

—

E_{2}^{r,s}(\kappa_{d,4}/\mathcal{S}_{4})
is an isomorphism for all
(r,s)\in\{0,\ldots,d-2\}\times\{0,\ldots,d-1\}
,

—

E_{2}^{r,s}(\kappa_{d,4}/\mathcal{S}_{4})
is an epimorphism for all
(r,s)\in\{0,\ldots,d-2\}\times\{0,\ldots,2(d-1)\}
,

—

E_{2}^{r,s}(\kappa_{d,4}/\mathcal{S}_{4})\neq 0
is a monomorphism for all
(r,s)\in\{d-1\}\times\{1,\ldots,d-1\}
,

—

E_{2}^{r,s}(\kappa_{d,4}/\mathcal{S}_{4})=0
for every
(r,s)\notin\{0,\ldots,d-1\}\times\{0,\ldots,2(d-1)\}
, because all
E_{2}^{r,s}(d)
vanish.

(For an illustration of the morphism
E_{2}^{r,s}(\kappa_{d,n})
see Figure
7
.)
Thus, if we prove that the spectral sequence (
16
) collapses at the
E_{2}
-term the same will be true for the spectral sequence (
15
).
Indeed, in the next section we prove Theorem
3.4
which guaranties that the spectral sequence (
16
) collapses at the
E_{2}
-term.
Consequently, the spectral sequence (
15
) also collapses at the
E_{2}
-term because all the differentials emanating from positions
(d-1,s)\in\{d-1\}\times\mathbb{Z}
are zero.

Now, the calculation of the cohomology of the real projective space with local coefficients
H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{M})
, presented in Section
7.4.4
of the appendix, in combination with Lemma
3.2
from the next section, gives the complete description of the
E_{2}
-terms of the both spectral sequences.

—

For the spectral sequence (
15
) we have

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0ptE_{2}^{r,s}(d)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}H^{0}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\oplus q(s)},&r=0,s\text{ even, }0\leq s\leq 2d-2,\\
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\oplus q(s)},&r=0,s\text{ odd, }1\leq s\leq 2d-3,\\
H^{d-1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\oplus q(s)},&r=d-1,s\text{ even, }0\leq s\leq 2d-2,\\
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\oplus q(s)},&r=d-1,s\text{ odd, }1\leq s\leq 2d-3,\\
H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2}),&1\leq r\leq d-2,s\text{ even, }0\leq s\leq 2d-2,\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

where
q(s):=|\{(i,j)\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}:0\leq i<j\leq d-1,i+j=s\}|
.

—

For the spectral sequence (
16
) we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{r}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathcal{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathbb{F}_{2}))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle H^{r}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};H^{s}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}\times\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathbb{F}_{2}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}H^{0}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\oplus q(s)},&r=0,s\text{ is even, }s\geq 0,\\
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\oplus q(s)},&r=0,s\text{ is odd, }s\geq 1,\\
H^{r}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2}),&r\geq 1,s\text{ is even, }s\geq 0,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

In Figure
8
the
E_{2}
-term of the Serre spectral sequence (
15
) associated with the fibration (
13
) in the case
d=5
is presented.

Figure 8.
E_{2}=E_{\infty}
-term of the Serre spectral sequence (
15
) for the fibration (
13
) when
d=5
.
In the picture, for example, by
e_{1}\otimes 1\in E_{2}^{0,1}
we denote the invariant element
e_{1}\otimes 1+1\otimes e_{1}
.

The additive basis
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)
for the cohomology
H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)/\mathcal{S}_{4};\mathbb{F}_{2})
is specified using already defined basis
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)
in the following way:

(a)

(e^{i}\otimes e^{i})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f^{k}=(e^{i}_{1}\otimes e^{i}_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f^{k}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)
for
e^{i}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)
,
0\leq k\leq d-1
,

(b)

(e^{i}\otimes e^{j})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1=(e^{i}_{1}\otimes e^{j}_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)
for
e^{i},e^{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)
,
0\leq j<i\leq d-1
,

(c)

(e^{i}\otimes e^{j})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}z_{d-1}=(e^{i}_{1}\otimes e^{j}_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}z_{d-1}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)
when
d<\infty
for
e^{i},e^{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)
,
0\leq j<i\leq d-1
.

Here
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
denotes the multiplicative generator of the cohomology of the base space of the fibration (
13
).
Moreover, in the definition of the basis
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)
we used two notations.
One of them is analogues to the notation used in Section
7.4.4
for the generator
(e^{i}\otimes e^{j})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}z_{d-1}
of the cohomology group
H^{d-1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{M}_{i,j})
where the local coefficient system
\mathcal{M}_{i,j}\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}
is given by cohomology classes
e^{i}\otimes e^{j}
and
e^{j}\otimes e^{i}
.

Now, a partition of the basis
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)
is defined by

\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4):=\big\{(x\otimes x)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f^{k}:x\in\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2),\,0\leq k\leq d-1\big\}=\\
\big\{(e^{i}\otimes e^{i})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f^{k}:e^{i}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2),\,0\leq k\leq d-1\big\},

and in addition

\mathcal{B}_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4):=\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4){\setminus}\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)=\\
\big\{(e^{i}\otimes e^{j})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1:e^{i},e^{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2),\ 0\leq j<i\leq d-1\big\}\cup\\
\big\{(e^{i}\otimes e^{j})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}z_{d-1}:d<\infty,\ 0\leq j<i\leq d-1\big\}.

Furthermore, denote by

\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4):=\langle\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)\rangle\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4):=\langle\mathcal{B}_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)\rangle.

Then there is an additive decomposition of the cohomology of
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)/\mathcal{S}_{4}
as follows:

H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)/\mathcal{S}_{4};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)\oplus\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4),

where
\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)
turns out to be a subalgebra of
H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)/\mathcal{S}_{4};\mathbb{F}_{2})
while
\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)
is an ideal.
More precisely, there is an isomorphism of algebras

\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)\cong\langle(1\otimes 1)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f,(e_{1}\otimes e_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1\rangle\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{2,1},V_{2,2}]/\langle V_{2,1}^{d},V_{2,2}^{d}\rangle,

where
V_{2,1}=(1\otimes 1)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f
and
V_{2,2}=(e\otimes e)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1=(e_{1}\otimes e_{2})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1
.
Thus, we have proved that the cohomology
H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)/\mathcal{S}_{4};\mathbb{F}_{2})
can be decomposed into the sum of a subalgebra and an ideal as follows

H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4)/\mathcal{S}_{4};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{2,1},V_{2,2}]/\langle V_{2,1}^{d},V_{2,2}^{d}\rangle\oplus\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},4),

(17)

where
\deg(V_{2,1})=2^{1-1}=1
and
\deg(V_{2,2})=2^{2-1}=2
.
The part of the Serre spectral sequence in the case
d=5
induced by the subalgebra
\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{5},4)
is illustrated in Figure
9
.

Figure 9.
The algebra
\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{5},4)
.

3.3.
Cohomology of
(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}
and
(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}

Let
X
be a CW-complex (not necessarily finite dimensional).
Consider an action of the group
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\langle t\rangle
on the product
X\times X
given by
t\cdot(x_{1},x_{2}):=(x_{2},x_{1})
.
Then the product spaces
(X\times X)\times S^{d-1}
and
(X\times X)\times\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
are equipped with the diagonal
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-action where the action on
\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
comes with the space definition and the action on
S^{d-1}
is assumed to be antipodal.
In this section, using the classical work of Minoru Nakaoka
[
87
]
and following the fundamental book of Alejandro Adem and James Milgram
[
2
, Sec. IV.1]
, we describe the cohomology with
\mathbb{F}_{2}
-coefficients of the following quotient spaces

\displaystyle(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}

\displaystyle:=\big((X\times X)\times S^{d-1}\big)/\mathbb{Z}_{2},

\displaystyle(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\big((X\times X)\times\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\big)/\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

For this we will use Serre spectral sequences of the following fibrations.

The spaces
(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}
and
(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
are the total spaces of the following fiber bundles

\textstyle{X\times X\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ (X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1},}

(18)

where we use that
S^{d-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\cong\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}
, and

\textstyle{X\times X\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ (X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}\ \cong\ \mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2}.}

(19)

If for a model of
\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
we take
S^{\infty}
with antipodal action the natural inclusion map
S^{d-1}\longrightarrow S^{\infty}
induces the following map between corresponding quotient spaces

\textstyle{(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{d}}
\textstyle{\ (X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{\infty}\cong(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}.}

(20)

This map induces a morphism of the fiber bundles (
18
) and (
19
):

\textstyle{(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{d}}
\textstyle{\ (X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{\infty}\cong(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}\cong\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2}.}

(21)

The cohomology of the total spaces of both fibrations (
18
) and (
19
) can be computed using Serre spectral sequences.
In the case of the fibration (
18
) the
E_{2}
-term of the associated Serre spectral sequence is of the form

E_{2}^{r,s}(d)=H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{H}^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})).

(22)

The local coefficient system
\mathcal{H}^{*}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
is determined by the action of the fundamental group
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})
of the base space on the cohomology of the fiber
H^{*}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
Recall that
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})
is a cyclic group.
In particular, the Künneth formula
[
21
, Thm. VI.3.2]
gives a presentation of the cohomology of the fiber

H^{*}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H^{*}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})\otimes H^{*}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2}),

and the action of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})
is given by the cyclic shift of factors in the tensor product.

The Serre spectral sequence associated to the fibration (
19
) has the
E_{2}
-term

\displaystyle E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)

\displaystyle=H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathcal{H}^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\cong H^{r}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathcal{H}^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))

(23)

\displaystyle\cong H^{r}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};H^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})).

The fundamental group of the base
\pi_{1}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
acts on the cohomology of the fiber and defines the local coefficient system
\mathcal{H}^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
, or defines the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-module structure on
H^{r}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};H^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))
.

The morphism (
21
) between the fibrations (
18
) and (
19
) induces a morphism between the spectral sequences (
22
) and (
23
):

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 82.31696pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-82.31696pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E_{2}^{r,s}(d)=H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{H}^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\ }$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 106.31696pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)=H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathcal{H}^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.0265pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{E_{2}^{r,s}(i_{d})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.31697pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The homomorphisms
E_{2}^{r,s}(i_{d})
are isomorphisms whenever
E_{2}^{r,s}(d)\neq 0
and
(r,s)\notin\{d-1\}\times\mathbb{Z}
, or
E_{2}^{r,s}(d)=E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)=0
.
In the case when
(r,s)\in\{d-1\}\times\mathbb{Z}
we have that

E_{2}^{d-1,s}(d)\cong E_{2}^{d-1,s}(\infty)\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}^{b(d,X)},

where for
s
odd

b(d,X)=\sum_{0\leq i<j,\,i+j=s}\big(\mathrm{rank}(H^{i}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\cdot\mathrm{rank}(H^{j}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\big),

and for
s
even

b(d,X)=\sum_{0\leq i<j,\,i+j=s}\big(\mathrm{rank}(H^{i}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\cdot\mathrm{rank}(H^{j}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\big)+\\
\frac{1}{2}\big(\mathrm{rank}(H^{\tfrac{s}{2}}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\big)^{2}-\mathrm{rank}(H^{\tfrac{s}{2}}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})).

Consult also Section
7.4.4
.
In particular, all the homomorphisms
E_{2}^{r,s}(i_{d})
are monomorphism, and for
(r,s)\notin\{d-1\}\times\mathbb{Z}
they are non-zero epimorphisms.

To make any further progress in computation of the spectral sequences (
22
) and (
23
) we need to understand the action of the fundamental groups of the base spaces on the cohomology of the fibers.
For that we use the following lemma, which is a particular case of
[
2
, Lem. IV.1.4]
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
V=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}V_{n}
be a graded
\mathbb{F}_{2}
vector space, and let
\mathcal{B}:=\{v_{i}:i\in I\}
be a basis of
V
where the index set
I
is equipped with a linear order.
The tensor product vector space
V\otimes V
as a
\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
-module, where the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is given by the cyclic shift, is a direct sum of free and trivial
\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
-modules.
The trivial modules are generated by the elements of the form
v_{i}\otimes v_{i}
where
v_{i}
is an element of the basis
\mathcal{B}
, while the free modules are generated by the elements of the form
v_{i}\otimes v_{j}
where
i<j
and
v_{i},v_{j}
belong to
\mathcal{B}
.

Proof.

A basis of the vector space
V\otimes V
is given by all vectors of the form
v_{i}\otimes v_{j}
where
v_{i},v_{j}\in\mathcal{B}
.
The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\langle t\rangle
-action on
V\otimes V
preserves this basis.
Since
t\cdot(v_{i}\otimes v_{j})=v_{j}\otimes v_{i}
we have that each element
v_{i}\otimes v_{i}
generates a copy of the trivial
\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
-module and each element
v_{i}\otimes v_{j}
,
i<j
, generates a copy of the free
\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
-module.
Thus,
V\otimes V
is a direct sum of free and trivial
\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
-modules.
∎

Since the homomorphisms
E_{2}^{r,s}(i_{d})
are isomorphisms for
(r,s)\in\{0,\ldots,d-2\}\times\mathbb{Z}
, monomorphism when
(r,s)\in\{d-1\}\times\mathbb{Z}
, and otherwise zero homomorphisms, we study first the spectral sequence (
23
).
If we prove that
E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)=E_{\infty}^{r,s}(\infty)
it would imply that all its differentials vanish and consequently the same would hold for the spectral sequence (
22
) implying that
E_{2}^{r,s}(d)=E_{\infty}^{r,s}(d)
.
In the next step we describe the
E_{2}
-term of the spectral sequence (
23
), see also
[
2
, Cor. IV.1.6]
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{B}:=\{v_{i}:i\in I\}
be a basis of the
\mathbb{F}_{2}
vector space
H^{*}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
where the index set
I
is equipped with a linear order.
The
E_{2}
-term of the spectral sequence (
23
) can be presented as follows:

\displaystyle E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)

\displaystyle=H^{r}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathcal{H}^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\cong H^{r}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};H^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))

\displaystyle\cong\begin{cases}H^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}},&r=0,\\
H^{\frac{s}{2}}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2}),&r>0,\,s\text{ \rm even},\\
0,&\text{\rm otherwise}.\end{cases}

Moreover,
E_{2}^{*,*}(\infty)
as a
H^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})
-module, ignoring the grading, decomposes into the direct sum

\bigoplus_{i\in I}H^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})\oplus\bigoplus_{i<j\in I}\mathbb{F}_{2}

where the action of
H^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})
on each summand of the first sum is given by the cup product, and on the each summand of the second sum is trivial.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Lemma
3.1
, and the facts that:

—

H^{0}(G;M)=M^{G}
for any group
G
and any
G
-module
M
, and

—

H^{i}(G;F)=0
for
i\geq 1
when
F
is a projective (free)
G
-module.

∎

Now, for the spectral sequence (
22
), using the calculation presented in Section
7.4.4
, we get the following presentation of the corresponding
E_{2}
-term.

Lemma 3.3
.

The the
E_{2}
-term of the spectral sequence (
22
) can be presented as follows

\displaystyle E_{2}^{r,s}(d)

\displaystyle=H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{H}^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))

\displaystyle\cong\begin{cases}H^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}},&r\in\{0,d-1\},\\
H^{\frac{s}{2}}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2}),&1\leq r\leq d-2,\,s\text{ \rm even},\\
0,&\text{\rm otherwise}.\end{cases}

The fact that the
E_{2}
-term of the spectral sequence (
23
) collapses dates back to work of
Smith
[
93
]
, Steenrod
[
95
]
and
Nakaoka
[
87
]
,
for a result in more general setup consult more recent work of Leary
[
71
, Thm. 2.1]
.
The following theorem is a special case of
[
2
, Thm. IV.1.7]
.

Theorem 3.4
.

Let
X
be a CW-complex.
The Serre spectral sequence of the fibration

\textstyle{X\times X\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ (X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2}.}

(24)

collapses at the
E_{2}
-term, that is,
E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)\cong E_{\infty}^{r,s}(\infty)
for all
(r,s)\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}:=\{v_{i}:i\in I\}
be a basis of the
\mathbb{F}_{2}
vector space
H^{*}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
where the index set
I
is equipped with a linear order.
The
E_{2}
-term (
23
) of the Serre spectral sequence of the fibration (
24
) is calculated in Lemma
3.2
.
Further on, the
\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
-module structure on the cohomology of the fiber – the coefficient system – is described in Lemma
3.1
.
Since the differentials of these spectral sequences are
H^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})
-module maps we concentrate on the generators of the
H^{*}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})
-module structure of the rows of the spectral sequences.
In this situation it means that we consider elements of the zero column and prove that they survive to the
E_{\infty}
-term.
Consequently the proof of the theorem proceeds in two steps.

(A)

Let
v_{i}
and
v_{j}
be two different cohomology classes from the basis
\mathcal{B}
.
In the first step we prove that all the elements (of the form
v_{i}\otimes v_{j}+v_{j}\otimes v_{i}
) in
E_{2}^{0,s}
associated to the invariants of free
\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
-modules (generated by
v_{i}\otimes v_{j}
) in the decomposition of
H^{*}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
survive to the
E_{\infty}
-term.
Since
\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is a contractible and free
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-space we have that
(X\times X)\times\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\simeq(X\times X)
is a free
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-space and the quotient map

\pi\colon(X\times X)\times\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\longrightarrow(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}

is a covering map.
Denote by
p\colon(X\times X)\times\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\longrightarrow X\times X
the projection.
Since it is a homotopy equivalence it induces an isomorphism in cohomology.
Furthermore, there is a transfer homomorphism

\mathrm{tr}\colon H^{*}((X\times X)\times\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}((X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})

with a property that that composition

(p^{*})^{-1}\circ\pi^{*}\circ\mathrm{tr}\circ p^{*}\colon H^{*}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})

is the map

v_{i}\otimes v_{j}\longmapsto v_{i}\otimes v_{j}+v_{j}\otimes v_{i}=(1+t)\cdot(v_{i}\otimes v_{j}),

where
v_{i},v_{j}\in H^{*}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
,
t
is a generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, and
1+t\in\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
.
Thus, the image
\operatorname{im}((p^{*})^{-1}\circ\pi^{*}\circ\mathrm{tr}\circ p^{*})
is contained in
H^{*}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})^{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
and each element is associated to an invariant element
v_{i}\otimes v_{j}+v_{j}\otimes v_{i}
, where
v_{i}\neq v_{j}
, of a free
\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
-module in the decomposition of the cohomology
H^{*}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
Since the composition
(p^{*})^{-1}\circ\pi^{*}\circ\mathrm{tr}\circ p^{*}
factors through the cohomology
H^{*}((X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})

all these elements survive to the
E_{\infty}
-term.

(B)

Let
v
be a cohomology classes of dimension
n
from the basis
\mathcal{B}
.
In the second step we prove that all the elements (of the form
v\otimes v
) in
E_{2}^{0,s}
associated to the invariants of trivial
\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
-modules (generated by
v\otimes v
) in the decomposition of
H^{*}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
survive to the
E_{\infty}
-term.
Using the bijective correspondence

H^{n}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})\longleftrightarrow[X,K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)]

(25)

we can present the cohomology class
v
as the image of the fundamental class
\iota_{n}\in H^{*}(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n);\mathbb{F}_{2})
along the map
\nu\colon X\longrightarrow K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)
that is associated to
v
via the correspondence (
25
), that is
v=\nu^{*}(\iota_{n})
.
For more details about the correspondence (
25
) consult for example
[
81
, Thm. 1, page 3]
.
The map
\nu
induces the following
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-equivariant map
\nu\times\nu\colon X\times X\longrightarrow K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)
, and consequently a morphism of Borel construction fibrations:

\textstyle{(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\nu\times\nu)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\ (K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2}.}

This morphism of fibrations induces a morphism between associated Serre spectral sequences.
In particular, the map between
E_{2}^{0,2n}
entries sends the class
\iota_{n}\otimes\iota_{n}
to the class
v\otimes v
, see Figure
10
.
Consequently, if the class
\iota_{n}\otimes\iota_{n}
survives to the
E_{\infty}
-term (all differentials evaluated at
\iota_{n}\otimes\iota_{n}
are zero), then the class
v\otimes v
also survives to the
E_{\infty}
-term.

Figure 10.
The morphism between Serre spectral sequences induced by the map
\operatorname{id}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}(\nu\times\nu)
.

Hence, we prove that the class
\iota_{n}\otimes\iota_{n}
survives to the
E_{\infty}
-term in the Serre spectral sequences associated to the Borel construction fibration

\textstyle{K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ (K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2}.}

(26)

The
E_{2}
-term of this spectral sequence is of the form

E_{2}^{r,s}=H^{r}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};H^{s}(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n);\mathbb{F}_{2})).

(27)

Since the Eilenberg--Mac Lane space
K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)
is
(n-1)
-connected the Künneth formula
[
21
, Thm. VI.3.2]
implies that

H^{s}(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n);\mathbb{F}_{2})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{F}_{2},&\text{for }s=0,\\
0,&\text{for }1\leq s\leq n-1,\\
\text{free }\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\text{-module},&\text{for }n\leq s\leq 2n-1,\\
\text{not relevant for our proof},&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Here we used that for
n\leq s\leq 2n-1
the following holds:

H^{s}(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n);\mathbb{F}_{2})=\\
\big(H^{s}(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n);\mathbb{F}_{2})\otimes H^{0}(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n);\mathbb{F}_{2})\big)\oplus\\
\big(H^{0}(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n);\mathbb{F}_{2})\otimes H^{s}(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n);\mathbb{F}_{2})\big).

Thus, a part of the
E_{2}
-term vanishes, meaning

E_{2}^{r,s}=H^{r}(\mathbb{Z}_{2};H^{s}(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n);\mathbb{F}_{2}))=0,

for all
r\geq 1
and
1\leq s\leq 2n-1
.
Therefore the element
\iota_{n}\otimes\iota_{n}\in E_{2}^{0,2n}
survives to the the
E_{\infty}
-term if and only if
\partial_{2n+1}(\iota_{n}\otimes\iota_{n})=0
.
It suffices to prove that no non-zero differential lands in the zero row of the spectral sequence.

The fibration (
26
) has a section

\sigma\colon\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\longrightarrow(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}

induced by the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-map
\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\longrightarrow(K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n))\times\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
given by
e\longmapsto(x_{0},x_{0},e)
where
e\in\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and
x_{0}\in K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)
is an arbitrary point that we fixed.
Consequently,
q\circ\sigma=\operatorname{id}_{\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2}}
.
Passing to cohomology we get
\sigma^{*}\circ q^{*}=\operatorname{id}_{H^{*}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})}
implying that

q^{*}\colon H^{*}(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}((K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n)\times K(\mathbb{Z}_{2},n))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})

is a monomorphism.
Hence, all the element of the zero row of the
E_{2}
-term of the spectral sequence (
27
) survive to the
E_{\infty}
-term, implying that all the differentials lending in the zero row vanish.
In particular, this means that
\partial_{2n+1}(\iota_{n}\otimes\iota_{n})=0
, and we concluded the proof of the theorem. ∎

Corollary 3.5
.

Let
X
be a CW-complex.
The Serre spectral sequence of the fibration

\textstyle{X\times X\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ (X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}}

collapses at the
E_{2}
-term, that is,
E_{2}^{r,s}(d)\cong E_{\infty}^{r,s}(d)
for all
(r,s)\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

We prove that all differentials of the spectral sequence
E_{*}^{*,*}(d)
vanish.
In (
20
), using
S^{\infty}
as a model for
\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, we have defined the map

i_{d}\colon(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\longrightarrow(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}S^{\infty}\cong(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

The map
i_{d}
induces a morphism of the fiber bundles:

\textstyle{(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{d}}
\textstyle{\ (X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{\infty}\cong(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty}\cong\mathrm{B}\mathbb{Z}_{2}.}

that in turn gives a morphism between the corresponding Serre spectral sequences:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 82.31696pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-82.31696pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E_{2}^{r,s}(d)=H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{H}^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\ }$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 106.31696pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)=H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{\infty};\mathcal{H}^{s}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.0265pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{E_{2}^{r,s}(i_{d})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.31697pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Since by Theorem
3.4
the spectral sequence
E_{2}^{r,s}(\infty)
collapses at the
E_{2}
-term all the differential of this spectral sequence vanish.
Consequently, all the elements in the image
\operatorname{im}\big(E_{2}^{r,s}(i_{d})\big)\subseteq E_{2}^{r,s}(d)
survive to the infinity term.
The only elements of
E_{2}^{r,s}(d)
not contained in
\operatorname{im}\big(E_{2}^{r,s}(i_{d})\big)
belong to the
(d-1)
-column, correspond to
\mathbb{F}_{2}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]
-free summands in the cohomology
H^{*}(X\times X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
, and are of the form
(\text{something})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}z_{d-1}
, consult Section
7.4.4
.
Because all differentials emanating from the
(d-1)
-column of the spectral sequence
E_{*}^{*,*}(d)
are zero and all differentials arriving at the
(d-1)
-column have to be zero we conclude that all differentials in
E_{*}^{*,*}(d)
indeed vanish.
∎

In summary, when an additive basis of the
\mathbb{F}_{2}
vector space
H^{*}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
is given,
then we can describe a basis of the cohomology of the spaces
(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}
and
(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathrm{E}\mathbb{Z}_{2}
as follows.
Keep in mind notation introduced in Section
7.4.4
.

Theorem 3.6
.

Let
d\geq 2
be integer or
d=\infty
, let
X
be a CW-complex, and let
\mathcal{B}_{X}
be an additive basis of
H^{*}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
Denote by
f^{j}
for
0\leq j\leq d-1
the additive generator of the group
H^{j}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}
, where
f\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
is the multiplicative generator of the cohomology ring
H^{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
Here we assume that
1=f^{0}\in H^{0}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
An additive basis
\mathcal{B}
of the cohomology

H^{*}((X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})

can be given the following way:

(1)

If
v\in\mathcal{B}_{X}
, then
(v\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f^{j}\in\mathcal{B}
for
0\leq j\leq d-1
with
\deg((v\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f^{j})=2\deg(v)+\deg(f^{j})=2\deg(v)+j
;

(2)

If
u,v\in\mathcal{B}_{X}
and
u\neq v
, then
(u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1\in\mathcal{B}
with
\deg((u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1)=\deg(u)+\deg(v)
; and

(3)

If
u,v\in\mathcal{B}_{X}
and
u\neq v
with
d<\infty
, then we set
(u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}z_{d-1}\in\mathcal{B}
where
\deg((u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}z_{d-1})=\deg(u)+\deg(v)+d-1
.

Proof.

The spectral sequences (
22
) and (
23
), depending whether
d<\infty
or
d=\infty
, converge to the cohomology
H^{*}((X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
Since by Theorem
3.4
and Corollary
3.5
both spectral sequences collapse at the
E_{2}
-term it suffices to find a basis of
E_{2}
-terms.
Thus, the proof is concluded by a direct application of Lemma
3.2
and Lemma
3.3
.
∎

The calculation of the cohomology of
(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}
we presented was done with coefficients in the field
\mathbb{F}_{2}
.
The Universal Coefficient theorem transcribes the arguments for cohomology into homology arguments, implying the following claim.

Theorem 3.7
.

Let
d\geq 2
be integer or
d=\infty
, let
X
be a CW-complex, and let
\mathcal{B}_{X}^{{}^{\prime}}
be an additive basis of the homology
H_{*}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
Denote by
f_{j}
for
0\leq j\leq d-1
the generator of the group
H_{j}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}
.
An additive basis
\mathcal{B}^{{}^{\prime}}
of the homology

H_{*}((X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})

can be given the following way:

(1)

If
v\in\mathcal{B}_{X}^{{}^{\prime}}
, then
(v\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f_{j}\in\mathcal{B}^{{}^{\prime}}
for
1\leq j\leq d-1
with
\deg((v\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f_{j})=2\deg(v)+\deg(f_{j})=2\deg(v)+j
;

(2)

If
u,v\in\mathcal{B}_{X}^{{}^{\prime}}
and
u\neq v
, then
(u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1\in\mathcal{B}
with
\deg((u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1)=\deg(u)+\deg(v)
; and

(3)

If
u,v\in\mathcal{B}_{X}^{{}^{\prime}}
and
u\neq v
with
d<\infty
, then we set
(u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}h_{d-1}\in\mathcal{B}^{{}^{\prime}}
where
\deg((u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}h_{d-1})=\deg(u)+\deg(v)+d-1
.

A useful consequence of Theorem
3.6
is the following fact.

Corollary 3.8
.

Let
d\geq 2
be integer or
d=\infty
, let
X
and
Y
be CW-complexes, and let
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
be a continuous map.
If the induced map in cohomology
f^{*}\colon H^{*}(Y;\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})
is injective, then the induced map

((f\times f)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id})^{*}\colon H^{*}((Y\times Y)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}((X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2}),

is also injective.

Proof.

The induced map
(f\times f)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id}\colon(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\longrightarrow(Y\times Y)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}
is covering the identity map in the following bundle morphism:

\textstyle{(X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(f\times f)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\ (Y\times Y)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S^{d-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\ S^{d-1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}.}

The bundle morphism induces the morphism between the corresponding Serre spectral sequences that corresponds to the homomorphism
((f\times f)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id})^{*}
.
Since both spectral sequences collapse at
E_{2}
-term and there is no extension problem Theorem
3.6
in combination with the assumption that
f^{*}
is injective yields the injectivity of
((f\times f)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id})^{*}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

The following dual version of the previous corollary also holds.

Corollary 3.9
.

Let
d\geq 2
be integer or
d=\infty
, let
X
and
Y
be CW-complexes, and let
f\colon X\longrightarrow Y
be a continuous map.
If the induced map in homology

f_{*}\colon H_{*}(X;\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H_{*}(Y;\mathbb{F}_{2})

is surjective, then the induced map

((f\times f)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id})_{*}\colon H^{*}((X\times X)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}((Y\times Y)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2}),

is also surjective.

3.4.
The induction step

In this section we take
d\geq 2
to be an integer or
d=\infty
.
Let us assume that for
m=k
the cohomology

H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})=H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

is determined by specifying a basis
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})
and its associated partition
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})=\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\cup\mathcal{B}_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})
in such a way that

\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}):=\langle\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\rangle\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{k,1},\ldots,V_{k,k}]/\langle V_{k,1}^{d},\ldots,V_{k,k}^{d}\rangle

is a subalgebra and
\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}):=\langle\mathcal{B}_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\rangle
is an ideal of
H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}};\mathbb{F}_{2})
, and in addition

\displaystyle H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\oplus\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{k,1},\ldots,V_{k,k}]/\langle V_{k,1}^{d},\ldots,V_{k,k}^{d}\rangle\oplus\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}),

(28)

where
\deg(V_{k,r})=2^{r-1}
for
1\leq r\leq k
.

Now, for
m=k+1
we study the cohomology

H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

According to Definition
2.1
we have that

\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})=(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\times\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}))\times S^{d-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}=(\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

Using the nature of the
\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}
-action on the spaces
\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
we have that

\displaystyle\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}

\displaystyle\cong\big((\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\times\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}))\times S^{d-1}\big)/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}}

\displaystyle\cong\big((\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}})\times S^{d-1}\big)/\mathbb{Z}_{2}

\displaystyle=:\big(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\times\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}}\big)\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}.

Since the additive basis
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})
for the cohomology

H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

is already fixed we can now use Theorem
3.6
and get the basis
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
for the cohomology

H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

as follows:

(i)

If
v\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})
, then for all
0\leq j\leq d-1

(v\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f^{j}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1}),

with
\deg((v\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f^{j})=2\deg(v)+\deg(f^{j})=2\deg(v)+j
;

(ii)

If
u,v\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})
and
u\neq v
, then

(u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1}),

with
\deg((u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1)=\deg(u)+\deg(v)
; and

(iii)

If
u,v\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})
and
u\neq v
and
d<\infty
, then

(u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}z_{d-1}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1}),

with
\deg((u\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}z_{d-1})=\deg(u)+\deg(v)+d-1
.

The partition of the basis
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
just introduced is given by

(v\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f^{j}\in\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1}),

for
v\in\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})
and
0\leq j\leq d-1
, that is,

\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1}):=\{(v\otimes v)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f^{j}:v\in\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k}),\ 0\leq j\leq d-1\}.

Then we set
\mathcal{B}_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})=\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1}){\setminus}\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
.
As before, we denote by

\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1}):=\langle\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})\rangle\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1}):=\langle\mathcal{B}_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})\rangle.

Then we have the following additive decomposition of the cohomology

H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})\oplus\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1}).

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
d\geq 2
be an integer or
d=\infty
.
Then
\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
is a subalgebra and
\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
is an ideal of the cohomology algebra
H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}};\mathbb{F}_{2})
.
Moreover,

\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{k+1,1},\ldots,V_{k+1,k+1}]/\langle V_{k+1,1}^{d},\ldots,V_{k+1,k+1}^{d}\rangle,

(29)

and
\deg(V_{k+1,r})=2^{r-1}
for
1\leq r\leq k+1
.

Proof.

Since the description of the cohomology
H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{k+1}};\mathbb{F}_{2})
is derived from a spectral sequence with appropriate multiplication structure it follows directly that
\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
is a subalgebra, and
\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k+1})
is an ideal, consult Theorem
3.6
.
It remains to establish the isomorphism (
29
).

Let us set

V_{k+1,1}:=(1\otimes 1)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}f,

(30)

and for all
2\leq r\leq k+1
let

V_{k+1,r}:=(V_{k,r-1}\otimes V_{k,r-1})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}1.

(31)

Then

\deg(V_{k+1,1})=2\deg(1)+\deg(f)=2\cdot 0+1=1,

and

\deg(V_{k+1,r})=2\deg(V_{k,r-1})+\deg(1)=2\cdot 2^{r-2}+0=2^{r-1}

for
2\leq r\leq k+1
.
Therefore, from the construction of the set
\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})
and the assumption about the structure of the subalgebra

\mathrm{A}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{k})\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{k,1},\ldots,V_{k,k}]/\langle V_{k,1}^{d},\ldots,V_{k,k}^{d}\rangle,

directly follows that the isomorphism (
29
) holds.
∎

The calculations of this section establish the following theorem.

Theorem 3.11
.

Let
d\geq 2
be an integer or
d=\infty
, and let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
Then

H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\\
\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{m,1},\ldots,V_{m,m}]/\langle V_{m,1}^{d},\ldots,V_{m,m}^{d}\rangle\oplus\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m}),

(32)

where
\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
is an ideal, and
\deg(V_{m,r})=2^{r-1}
for
1\leq r\leq m
.
In particular, for
d=\infty
we have

H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{m,1},\ldots,V_{m,m}]\oplus\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m}).

(33)

3.5.
The restriction homomorphisms

Let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
Consider the sequence of inclusions

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{m}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{2^{m}}}
\textstyle{\ \mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{O}(2^{m})}

where the last inclusion is the embedding give via the permutation representation.
The corresponding sequence of maps between classifying spaces

\textstyle{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{E}_{m}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{B}\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{B}\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{B}\mathrm{O}(2^{m})}

induces the following sequence of restriction homomorphisms:

\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathrm{O}(2^{m});\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{O}(2^{m})}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2}).}

In this section we study various aspects of these restriction homomorphisms.

3.5.1.

For
d=\infty
the isomorphism (
32
) gives the following decomposition of the cohomology of the group
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
:

\displaystyle H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\cong H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

(34)

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{m,1},\ldots,V_{m,m}]\oplus\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m}),

where
\deg(V_{m,r})=2^{r-1}
for
1\leq r\leq m
.

Recall that in Definition
2.7
we have specified the elementary abelian group
\mathcal{E}_{m}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\oplus m}
as a subgroup of
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
.
First we study the restriction map

\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\colon H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

From the definition of the basis
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
, its partition into subsetes
\mathcal{B}_{a}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
and
\mathcal{B}_{i}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
, and the definition of the element
V_{m,1}
follows that

V_{m,1}\cdot I^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})=0.

In addition, if we recall how we introduced the subgroup
\mathcal{E}_{m}
of
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
(see Definition
2.7
), and observe that multiplication by
\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(V_{m,1})
in
H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})
is injective, we can conclude that

\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m}))=0\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})\subseteq\ker(\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}).

(35)

Now, we shift our interest to the generators
V_{m,1},\ldots,V_{m,m}
of the polynomial subalgebra in the decomposition (
34
) and will identify its images under the restriction
\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(V_{m,1}),\ldots,\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(V_{m,m})
.
Even the definitions of the group
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
and its subgroup
\mathcal{E}_{m}
were inductive our approach to the description of the image of
\operatorname{im}(\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}})
is not be inductive.
For that we follow
[
64
, p. 266]
and first note that according to Lemma
7.23
the restriction image is contained in the ring of invariants of the corresponding Weyl group

\operatorname{im}\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\colon H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\big)\subseteq H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})^{W_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}(\mathcal{E}_{m})}\\
=\mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}]^{W_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}(\mathcal{E}_{m})}.

From Lemma
7.24
we know that
W_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}(\mathcal{E}_{m})=\mathrm{L}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
is the Sylow
2
-subgroup of
\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
of all lower triangular matrices with
1
’s on the main diagonal.
Therefore all polynomials

v_{m,1}:=\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(V_{m,1}),\ \ldots,\ v_{m,m}:=\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(V_{m,m})

are
\mathrm{L}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
invariant polynomials.
Each polynomial
v_{m,r}
has
y_{m-r+1}
as a factor by (
30
) and (
31
),
\deg(v_{m,r})=2^{r-1}
, and
v_{m,r}
is
\mathrm{L}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
invariant, consequently

v_{m,r}=\prod_{(\lambda_{m},\ldots,\lambda_{m-r+2})\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{r-1}}\big(\lambda_{m}\,y_{m}+\cdots+\lambda_{m-r+2}\,y_{m-r+2}+y_{m-r+1}\big),

(36)

as stated in
[
62
, (2.14)]
.
In particular, the polynomials
v_{m,1},\ldots,v_{m,m}
are algebraically independent.
In the notation of Theorem
7.12
we have that
v_{m,r}=h_{r}
, with identifications
m=n
and
y_{1}=x_{1},\ldots,y_{m}=x_{m}
.

3.5.2.

Let us now consider the restriction homomorphism

\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\colon H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

Like in the previous case using Lemma
7.23
we get that

\operatorname{im}\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\colon H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\big)\subseteq H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})^{W_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}(\mathcal{E}_{m})}\\
=\mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}]^{W_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}(\mathcal{E}_{m})}.

From Lemma
7.25
we get that
W_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}(\mathcal{E}_{m})\cong\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
, and consequently

\operatorname{im}\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\colon H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\big)\subseteq H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})^{W_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}(\mathcal{E}_{m})}\\
