=\mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}]^{\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})}.

Now the description of the ring of invariants given in Theorem
7.13
yields that

\operatorname{im}\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\colon H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\big)\subseteq H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})^{W_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}(\mathcal{E}_{m})}\\
=\mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}]^{\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})}=\mathbb{F}_{2}[d_{m,0},\ldots,d_{m,m-1}],

(37)

where
d_{m,0},\ldots,d_{m,m-1}
are the Dickson invariants.

Next we recall that

H^{*}(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{O}(2^{m});\mathbb{F}_{2})=\mathbb{F}[w_{1},\ldots,w_{2^{m}}],

where
w_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq 2^{m}
, denotes the
i
th Stiefel--Whitney class of the tautological vector bundle
\gamma_{2^{m}}
over
\mathrm{B}\mathrm{O}(2^{m})
, see
[
80
, Thm. 7.1]
.
Let us introduce the following notation

\textstyle{w_{2^{m}-2^{r}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{O}(2^{m})}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}}
\textstyle{\ w_{m,r}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}}
\textstyle{\ D_{m,r}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}}
\textstyle{\ d_{m,r},}

where
0\leq r\leq m-1
.
From Theorem
7.15
we have that indeed the classes
d_{m,0},\ldots,d_{m,m-1}
are Dickson invariants, and furthermore

\operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{O}(2^{m})}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(w_{i})=\begin{cases}d_{m,r}=\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(w_{m,r}),&i=2^{m}-2^{r},\ 0\leq r\leq m-1,\\
1,&i=0,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Hence, from (
37
) we conclude that

\operatorname{im}\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\colon H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\big)=H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})^{W_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}(\mathcal{E}_{m})}\\
=\mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}]^{\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})}=\mathbb{F}_{2}[d_{m,0},\ldots,d_{m,m-1}].

To summarise, we have proved the following lemma.

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
Then

\operatorname{im}\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\colon H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\big)=\mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}]^{\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})}\\
=\mathbb{F}_{2}[d_{m,0},\ldots,d_{m,m-1}],

(38)

where
d_{m,0},\ldots,d_{m,m-1}
are the Dickson invariants.
Consequently,

\displaystyle H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{im}\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\colon H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\big)\oplus\ker(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}})

(39)

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[d_{m,0},\ldots,d_{m,m-1}]\oplus\ker(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}})

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[w_{m,0},\ldots,w_{m,m-1}]\oplus\ker(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}).

Let us reflect on the facts we obtained so far.
For
0\leq r\leq m-1
we specified the following elements under the restriction maps:

\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathrm{O}(2^{m});\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{O}(2^{m})}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})}
\textstyle{w_{2^{m}-2^{r}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{O}(2^{m})}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}}
\textstyle{\ w_{m,r}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}}
\textstyle{\ D_{m,r}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}}
\textstyle{\ d_{m,r}}
\textstyle{V_{m,r+1}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}}
\textstyle{\ v_{m,r+1}.}

(40)

\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathrm{O}(2^{m});\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{O}(2^{m})}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{2}[w_{1},\dots,w_{2^{m}}]\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}_{2}[w_{m,0},\dots,w_{m,m-1}]\oplus\ker(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{m,1},\dots,V_{m,m}]\oplus I^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\dots,y_{m}]}
\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})^{\mathrm{L}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})}\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[v_{m,1},\dots,v_{m,m}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})^{\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})}\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[d_{m,0},\ldots,d_{m,m-1}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(41)

Furthermore, we have proved the factorizations of the restriction homomorphism which are described by the diagram (
41
)
on the next page.
In other words,

—

the restriction homomorphism
\operatorname{res}^{\mathrm{O}(2^{m})}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}
factors as follows:

\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathrm{O}(2^{m});\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})^{\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{2}[w_{1},\dots,w_{2^{m}}]\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}_{2}[d_{m,0},\dots,d_{m,m-1}]\ \ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}[y_{1},\dots,y_{m}],}

—

the restriction homomorphism
\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}
factors as follows

\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})^{\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ \ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{2}[w_{m,0},\dots,w_{m,m-1}]\oplus\ker(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}_{2}[d_{m,0},\dots,d_{m,m-1}]\ \ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}[y_{1},\dots,y_{m}],}

—

the restriction homomorphism
\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}
factors as follows:

\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})^{\mathrm{L}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ \ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{m,1},\dots,V_{m,m}]\oplus I^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}_{2}[v_{m,1},\dots,v_{m,m}]\ \ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{F}[y_{1},\dots,y_{m}].}

Now from Proposition
7.14
we get a connection between the Dickson invariant polynomials
d_{m,0},\ldots,d_{m,m-1}
and the
\mathrm{L}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
-invariant polynomials
v_{m,1},\ldots,v_{m,m}
.
For
0\leq r\leq m-1
and
d_{m-1,-1}=0
holds:

d_{m,r}=(\chi_{m}d_{m-1,r})\,v_{m,m}+(\chi_{m}d_{m-1,r-1})^{2}.

(42)

Here
\chi_{m}\in\operatorname{GL}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
is the variable change given by the matrix

\begin{pmatrix}0&0&\cdots&0&1\\
0&0&\cdots&1&0\\
&&\cdots&&\\
0&1&\cdots&0&0\\
1&0&\cdots&0&0\end{pmatrix}.

3.5.3.

In the final part of this section we prove the following two lemmas.
For the next lemma see also
[
64
, Lem. 3.14]
.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
Then

\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}\big(\langle w_{m,0}\rangle\big)\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{m,1},\ldots,V_{m,m}],

(43)

where
\langle w_{m,0}\rangle
denotes the principal ideal generated by the class
w_{m,0}
in
H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})
.

Proof.

Recall that in (
34
) we concluded that

H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{m,1},\ldots,V_{m,m}]\oplus\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m}).

In order to prove (
43
) it suffices to show that

D_{m,0}\cdot\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})=0,\qquad\text{and}\qquad D_{m,0}=V_{m,1}\cdots V_{m,m}.

(44)

Indeed, if the equalities (
44
) hold, and because
\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}(w_{m,0})=D_{m,0}
, we have that

\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}\big(\langle w_{m,0}\rangle\big)=\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}\big(w_{m,0}\cdot H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\big)\\
\subseteq D_{m,0}\cdot H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})=D_{m,0}\cdot\big(\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{m,1},\ldots,V_{m,m}]\oplus\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})\big)\\
=D_{m,0}\cdot\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{m,1},\ldots,V_{m,m}]\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{m,1},\ldots,V_{m,m}].

Thus, in order to finish the proof of the lemma it remains to verify equalities (
44
).

First we verify that
D_{m,0}\cdot\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})=0
.
For that we use a classical result of Quillen
[
90
]
about detection of group cohomology, see Section
7.4.1
.
In particular, according to Theorem
7.20
we have that the cohomology
H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})
of the Sylow
2
-subgroup
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
modulo
\mathbb{F}_{2}
is detected by the subgroups
\mathcal{E}_{m}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}
.
From (
35
) we have that
\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m}))=0
.
Consequently, if we prove that
\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}}(D_{m,0})=0
it would follow that
D_{m,0}\cdot\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})=0
.
To see that this restriction of
D_{m,0}
vanishes we first recall that
D_{m,0}
is a
(2^{m}-1)
-Stiefel--Whitney class of the vector bundle
\eta_{2^{m}}
:

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{E}\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\times_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{B}\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}},}

see Section
7.2.2
.
The vector bundle
\eta_{2^{m}}
can be decomposed into a Whitney sum of two vector bundles where one of them is a trivial line bundle.
Consequently, the
2^{m}
-Stiefel–Whitney class of the bundle vanishes.
The trivial line subbundle is determined by the trivial
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
subrepresentation
\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2^{m}})\in\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}:x_{1}=\cdots=x_{2^{m}}\}
of
\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}
.
Using the naturality property of Stiefel–Whitney classes
[
80
, Ax. 2, p. 35]
we have that
\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}}(D_{m,0})
is the
(2^{m}-1)
-Stiefel–Whitney class of the pull-back vector bundle:

\textstyle{\mathrm{E}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}})\times_{(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}})}\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega_{2^{m}}}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{E}\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\times_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{2^{m}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{B}\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}.}

The pull-back vector bundle
\omega_{2^{m}}
can be decomposed into a Whitney sum of two vector bundles where one of them is a two dimensional trivial vector bundle.
This trivial vector subbundle is determined by the trivial
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}
subrepresentation
\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2^{m}})\in\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}:x_{1}=\cdots=x_{2^{m-1}},\,x_{2^{m-1}+1}=\cdots=x_{2^{m}}\}
of
\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}
.
Hence,
(2^{m}-1)
-Stiefel--Whitney class of this bundle
w_{2^{m}-1}(\omega_{2^{m}})=\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}}(D_{m,0})
has to vanish.
This completes the proof of the first equality
D_{m,0}\cdot\mathrm{I}^{*}(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{m})=0
in (
44
).

Next we prove that
D_{m,0}=V_{m,1}\cdots V_{m,m}
.
Once again we use the fact that
H^{*}(\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})
is detected by the subgroups
\mathcal{E}_{m}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}
.
Since we showed that
\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}}(D_{m,0})=0
it suffices to show that

\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(D_{m,0})=\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(V_{m,1}\cdots V_{m,m})\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad d_{m,0}=v_{m,1}\cdots v_{m,m}.

Indeed, the equality
d_{m,0}=v_{m,1}\cdots v_{m,m}
can be established by direct computation using the induction on
m
in combination with relations (
36
) and (
42
), and observation that

\chi_{m}(v_{m-1,1}\cdots v_{m-1,m-1})=v_{m,1}\cdots v_{m,m-1}.

Hence, we showed that
D_{m,0}=V_{m,1}\cdots V_{m,m}
and consequently verification of the second equality in (
44
).
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

In the final lemma of this section we describe the kernel of the restriction homomorphism
\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}
.

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
Then

\ker\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}\big)=\langle w_{m,0}\rangle\subseteq H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

(45)

Proof.

For the proof of the lemma we use again a classical result of Quillen on the detection of group cohomology, see Section
7.4.1
.
From Theorem
7.20
we have that the cohomology
H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})
of the symmetric group
\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}
modulo
\mathbb{F}_{2}
is detected by the subgroups
\mathcal{E}_{m}
and
\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}
.
This means that the homomorphism

\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}\times\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{m};\mathbb{F}_{2})\times H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}};\mathbb{F}_{2})}

is a monomorphism.
Thus,

0\neq x\in\ker\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}\big)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{m}}(x)\neq 0.

Further on, using the decomposition (
39
) we get the implication

0\neq x\in\ker\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}\big)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad x\in\langle w_{m,0},\ldots,w_{m,m-1}\rangle.

Like in the proof of the previous lemma we consider the vector bundle
\xi_{2^{m}}
and its pull-back
\theta_{2^{m}}
introduced by the following pull-back diagram:

\textstyle{\mathrm{E}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})\times_{(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})}\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{2^{m}}}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{E}\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}\times_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi_{2^{m}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{B}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{B}\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}.}

As we know the classes
w_{m,0},\ldots,w_{m,m-1}
are the Stiefel--Whitney classes of the vector bundle
\xi_{2^{m}}
in dimensions
2^{m}-2^{0},\ldots,2^{m}-2^{m-1}
, respectively.
The pull-back vector bundle
\theta_{2^{m}}
can be decomposed into a Whitney sum of two vector bundles where one of them is a two dimensional trivial vector bundle.
The trivial vector subbundle is determined by the trivial
\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}
subrepresentation
\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{2^{m}})\in\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}:x_{1}=\cdots=x_{2^{m-1}},\,x_{2^{m-1}+1}=\cdots=x_{2^{m}}\}
of
\mathbb{R}^{2^{m}}
.
Hence,
(2^{m}-1)
-Stiefel–Whitney class of this bundle
\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}(w_{m,0})
has to vanish, or equivalently

w_{m,0}\in\ker\big(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}\big).

On the other hand the pull-back vector bundle
\theta_{2^{m}}
is isomorphic to the vector bundle
\xi_{2^{m-1}}\times\xi_{2^{m-1}}
.
Therefore,

\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}\big(w(\xi_{2^{m}})\big)=w(\theta_{2^{m}})=w(\xi_{2^{m-1}}\times\xi_{2^{m-1}})=w(\xi_{2^{m-1}})\times w(\xi_{2^{m-1}}),

and consequently for
2\leq r\leq m-1
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}(w_{m,r})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}(w_{2^{m}-2^{r}}(\xi_{2^{m}}))

\displaystyle=w_{2^{m}-2^{r}}(\xi_{2^{m-1}}\times\xi_{2^{m-1}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-2^{r}}w_{i}(\xi_{2^{m-1}})\times w_{2^{m}-2^{r}-i}(\xi_{2^{m-1}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-2^{r}}w_{i}(\xi_{2^{m-1}})\otimes w_{2^{m}-2^{r}-i}(\xi_{2^{m-1}})

\displaystyle\in\bigoplus_{i=0}^{2^{m}-2^{r}}H^{i}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\otimes H^{2^{m}-2^{r}-i}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

Here we silently use the Eilenberg--Zilber isomorphism
[
21
, Th. VI.3.2]
.
In particular, we can isolate a concrete (direct) summand in the decomposition as follows:

\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}(w_{m,r})=\sum_{i=0}^{2^{m}-2^{r}}w_{i}(\xi_{2^{m-1}})\otimes w_{2^{m}-2^{r}-i}(\xi_{2^{m-1}})=\\
w_{2^{m-1}-2^{r-1}}(\xi_{2^{m-1}})\otimes w_{2^{m-1}-2^{r-1}}(\xi_{2^{m-1}})+\\
\sum_{i\neq 2^{m-1}-2^{r-1}}w_{i}(\xi_{2^{m-1}})\otimes w_{2^{m}-2^{r}-i}(\xi_{2^{m-1}}).

Now we use the fact that the Stiefel--Whitney classes

w_{2^{m-1}-2^{m-2}}(\xi_{2^{m-1}}),\dots,w_{2^{m-1}-2^{0}}(\xi_{2^{m-1}})

are algebraically independent.
Indeed, they restrict to the corresponding Dickson invariants
d_{m-1,0},\dots,d_{m-1,m-2}
for which we know to be algebraically independent.
Consequently, the restricted homomorphism

\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}}|_{\langle w_{m,1},\ldots,w_{m,m-1}\rangle}

has to be a monomorphism.
Hence,

\ker(\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}})=\langle w_{m,0}\rangle
,
and the proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

4.
Hu’ng
’s Injectivity Theorem

Let
d\geq 2
be an integer or
d=\infty
, and let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
Consider the composition map
\rho_{d,2^{m}}:=\operatorname{id}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}\circ\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
between the quotient spaces

\textstyle{\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\ \ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}
\textstyle{\ \ \operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\ \ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}
\textstyle{\ \ \operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}},}

(46)

where the first map is induced by the
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
-equivariant map
\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}\colon\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})\longrightarrow\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})
, and the second map is induced by the identity.

The central objective of this section is to present a new and complete proof of the following claim, but first it is necessary to explain in detail several critical gaps in the published proof of this result,
[
62
, Thm. 3.1]
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
d\geq 2
be an integer or
d=\infty
, and let
m\geq 0
be an integer.
Then the homomorphism

\rho_{d,2^{m}}^{*}\colon H^{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

(47)

is a monomorphism.

Remark 4.2
.

The homomorphism
\rho_{d,2^{m}}^{*}
decomposes into the composition

(\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}})^{*}\circ(\operatorname{id}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}})^{*}.

Since the map
\operatorname{id}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}\colon\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\longrightarrow\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}
is a covering map then the composition homomorphism

\textstyle{H^{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\operatorname{id}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{[\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}:\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}]\cdot~}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{tr}}
\textstyle{H^{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})}

is the multiplication with the index
[\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}:\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}]
.
Here
\mathrm{tr}
denotes the classical transfer homomorphism, consult for example
[
60
, Sec. 3.G]
.
Since
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
is a Sylow
2
-subgroup the index
[\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}:\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}]
has to be odd.
Hence the composition
\mathrm{tr}\circ(\operatorname{id}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}})^{*}
is an isomorphism implying that
(\operatorname{id}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}})^{*}
is a monomorphism.
This means that in order to prove Theorem
4.1
it suffices to show that the homomorphism

\textstyle{(\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{m}}/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}})^{*}\colon H^{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})}

(48)

is a monomorphis.

Remark 4.3
.

In the case when
d=\infty
the homomorphism
\rho_{\infty,2^{m}}^{*}
becomes the restriction homomorphism
\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}
.
Since we are working in the field
\mathbb{F}_{2}
and
\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}
is a Sylow
2
-subgroup the restriction map
\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}}
is injective, see for example
[
23
, Prop. III.9.5(ii) and Thm. III.10.3]
.
Thus, Theorem
4.1
holds for
d=\infty
.

4.1.
Critical points in
Hu’ng
’s proof of his Injectivity Theorem

In order to simplify the comparison with the work of
Hu’ng
we begin with a dictionary that translates between our notation and the notation used in
[

]
.

The statement of Theorem
4.1
in
[
64
, Thm. 3.1]
is written as follows; the coefficient field
\mathbb{F}_{2}
is always to be assumed.

3.1. Theorem
i^{*}(q,n)\colon H^{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})\longrightarrow H^{*}(M(q,n))
is a monomorphism for
q\geq 1
,
n\geq 0
.

The proof of
[
64
, Thm. 3.1]
presented in
Hu’ng
’s paper is by induction on
n
.
It starts on the page 269 and ends on page 271.
This proof relies on
[
64
, Prop. 3.5]
.
The claim of
[
64
, Prop. 3.5]
is proven on page 275 and relies on
[

, Lem. 3.14]
and
[

, Lem. 3.19]
.

Now we outline the proof given by
Hu’ng
and exhibit two critical points that we have identified.
The proof is by induction on
n
.
For
n=0
the statement is easy to verify since
F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n})=\mathbb{R}^{q}
and
\widetilde{M}(q,n)=\mathrm{pt}
.
Let us assume that
i^{*}(q,n-1)
is injective.
Before we make the next step in the proof we define the maps
\mu_{m,n}
introduced in
[
64
, (3.2)]
, and the map
\varphi_{n-1}
defined in
[
64
, (2.3)]
.

For integers
m\geq 1
and
n\geq 1
consider the map

\textstyle{\mu_{m,n}\colon F(\mathbb{R}^{q},m)/\mathfrak{S}_{m}\times F(\mathbb{R}^{q},n)/\mathfrak{S}_{n}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ F(\mathbb{R}^{q},m+n)/\mathfrak{S}_{m+n},}

given by

\textstyle{[(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m})]\times[(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n})]\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ [(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m},y_{1}+z,\ldots,y_{n}+z)].}

Here for

R_{1}=\max_{1\leq k\leq m}\Big\|x_{k}-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}\Big\|\qquad\text{and}\qquad R_{2}=\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\Big\|y_{k}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{j}\Big\|

we define

z=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}x_{i}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}y_{j}-(R_{1}+R_{2}+1,0,\ldots,0).

Next for any
n\geq 1
we introduce the following map

\textstyle{\varphi_{n-1}\colon M(q,n-1)\times M(q,n-1)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M(q,n),}
\textstyle{(x,y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[(x,y,*)].}

Let us now consider the following diagram that commutes up to a homotopy

\textstyle{F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}\ }
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\big(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}}\big)^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\mu:=\mu_{2^{n-1},2^{n-1}}}
\textstyle{M(q,n)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{i(q,n)}
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces M(q,n-1)^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\varphi:=\varphi_{n-1}}
\scriptstyle{i(q,n-1)^{2}}

This diagram induces the following commutative diagram in cohomology where
Hu’ng
claimed that
each row is exact
I
I

I

The first critical point that is explained in Claim
4.4
.
:

\textstyle{0\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \ker(\mu^{*})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(q,n)|_{\ker(\mu^{*})}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(q,n)}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}((F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}})^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(q,n-1)^{2}}
\textstyle{\ 0}
\textstyle{0\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \ker(\varphi^{*})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(M(q,n))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{*}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(M(q,n-1)^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ 0}

(49)

From induction hypothesis we have that
i^{*}(q,n-1)^{2}=i^{*}(q,n-1)\otimes i^{*}(q,n-1)
is a monomorphism.
Then from
5
-lemma in order to conclude the induction, and consequently prove
[
64
, Thm. 3.1]
, it suffices to prove that

i^{*}(q,n)|_{\ker(\mu^{*})}\colon\ker(\mu^{*})\longrightarrow\ker(\varphi^{*})

is a monomorphism.

At this point we already crossed path with the first critical point in the proof (indicated by a footnote).
The following claim explains the nature of the problem that appears in the diagram (
49
).

Claim 4.4
.

For any integer
n\geq 2
, the map

\varphi^{*}\colon H^{*}(M(q,n))\longrightarrow H^{*}(M(q,n-1)^{2})

in (
49
) is not surjective.

Proof.

In the proof of the claim we use the notation of
Hu’ng
and keep in mind that

M(q,n)=\widetilde{M}(q,n)/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n},2}=\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{n}}.

The cohomology of this space is described in Section
3
.
For a reader convenience we repeat some of the arguments already presented.

From Definition
2.1
we have that

M(q,n)=\widetilde{M}(q,n)/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n},2}\cong(M(q,n-1)\times M(q,n-1))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{q-1}.

Since the action of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
on the sphere
S^{q-1}
is free the projection on the last coordinate induces the following fiber bundle

M(q,n-1)\times M(q,n-1)\longrightarrow\\
(M(q,n-1)\times M(q,n-1))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{q-1}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{q-1},

where the map
\varphi=\varphi_{n-1}
is the fiber embedding.

The Serre spectral sequence associated to this fibration has the
E_{2}
-term given by

E_{2}^{r,s}=H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{q-1};\mathcal{H}^{s}(M(q,n-1)\times M(q,n-1))).

As we have seen in Corollary
3.5
this spectral sequence collapses at the
E_{2}
-term, that is
E_{2}^{r,s}\cong E_{\infty}^{r,s}
.
In particular, for an arbitrary integer
k\geq 0
this means that the map
\varphi^{*}
factors as follows:

H^{k}(M(q,n))\cong\bigoplus_{r+s=k}E^{r,s}_{2}\longrightarrow\\
E^{0,k}_{2}\cong H^{k}(M(q,n-1)\times M(q,n-1))^{\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{q-1})}\\
\longrightarrow H^{k}(M(q,n-1)\times M(q,n-1)).

Here the first map is the projection and the second map is the inclusion.
It is important to recall that
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{q-1})
acts on
H^{s}(M(q,n-1)\times M(q,n-1))
by interchanging the factors in the product.
Thus, while the first map – the projection – is surjective, the second map is not surjective in all positive dimensions where
H^{k}(M(q,n-1)\times M(q,n-1))\neq 0
.
∎

Thus already at this point the proof of
[
64
, Thm. 3.1]
has the first problem.
Nevertheless, we continue to outline next steps of the proof that now concentrates on proving that

i^{*}(q,n)|_{\ker(\mu^{*})}\colon\ker(\mu^{*})\longrightarrow\ker(\varphi^{*})

is a monomorphism.
The complexity of the proof suggests that we first explain the strategy that was used by
Hu’ng
and then study particular details.
Consider the commutative diagram (
50
) on the next page,
which is an enrichment of the diagram (
49
) that we have already considered.

The proof of the injectivity of the map
i^{*}(q,n)|_{\ker(\mu^{*})}
presented by
Hu’ng
consists of several steps that we now list:

(A)

Description of
\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)
in terms of the dual Nakamura elements.

(B)

Description of
\ker(\mu^{*})
via the surjectivity of the map

i^{*}(F,q)|=i^{*}(F,q)|_{\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)}\colon\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\longrightarrow\ker(\mu^{*}).

(C)

Description of the image

(i^{*}(M,q)\circ i^{*}(\infty,n))\big(\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\big)\subseteq\ker(\varphi^{*}).

(D)

A proof that

\ker(\mu^{*})\cong(i^{*}(M,q)\circ\,i^{*}(\infty,n))\big(\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\big)

as
\mathbb{F}_{2}
-vector spaces.

\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}}^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(F,q)\mid}

\scriptstyle{i^{*}(\infty,n)\mid}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{n})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(F,q)}

\scriptstyle{i^{*}(\infty,n)}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}((F(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{n-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}})^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(F,q)^{2}}
\textstyle{0\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \ker(\mu^{*})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(q,n)|_{\ker(\mu^{*})}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(q,n)}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}((F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}})^{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(q,n-1)^{2}}
\textstyle{\ 0}
\textstyle{i^{*}(\infty,n)(\ker(\operatorname{res}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(M,q)\mid}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(M(\infty,n))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(M,q)}
\textstyle{0\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\varphi^{*}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}(M(q,n-1)^{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ 0}

(50)

These claims along with commutativity of the diagram (
50
) imply that the map

i^{*}(q,n)|_{\ker(\mu^{*})}\colon\ker(\mu^{*})\longrightarrow(i^{*}(M,q)\circ i^{*}(\infty,n))\big(\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\big)

is an isomorphism, and consequently

i^{*}(q,n)|_{\ker(\mu^{*})}\colon\ker(\mu^{*})\longrightarrow\ker(\varphi^{*})

is a monomorphism.
Thus, establishing claims we listed above would complete the proof of
[
64
, Thm. 3.1]
.
Now we discuss these steps separately exhibiting the second critical point of the proof.

(A)
and
(B)

For these steps multiple results of May
[
77
]
, Nakaoka
[
87
]
, Nakamura
[
85
]
, and
Hu’ng

[
62
]
are recalled and will be used in the proof.
For the reader’s convenience we collect the relevant facts as presented in
[
64
, Sec. 3]
.

First, the homology
H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},\infty)/\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})
can be identified with a Hopf subalgebra of the homology
H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},\infty)/\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})=H_{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})
,
[
76
, Sec. 5]
.
Furthermore,
H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},\infty)/\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})
is equipped with multiplicity in such a way that

{}_{m}H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},\infty)/\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})=H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},m)/\mathfrak{S}_{m},F(\mathbb{R}^{q},m-1)/\mathfrak{S}_{m-1}),

consult
[
62
, Sec. 2]
.
In general, an algebra
A
equipped with multiplicity has a decomposition
A=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\,{{}_{n}}A
, and we define its filtration by multiplicities with
A(m):=\bigoplus_{0\leq n\leq m}\,{{}_{n}}A
.
For more detailed definitions see
[
85
, p. 96]
.
In our concrete situation we have that

H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},\infty)/\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})(m)=H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},m)/\mathfrak{S}_{m}).

Let us further on, for integers
k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}\geq 0
, denote by

N_{k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}}\in H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},\infty)/\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})

the so called Nakamura element of multiplicity
2^{n}
, as introduced in
[
62
, Sec. 2]
.
Now we can quote the following theorem from
[
64
, Thm. 3.4]
.

3.4. Theorem
(Nakamura
[
85
]
, May
[
77
]
, Huýnh Múi
[
83
]
)

(i)

Let
q>0
and

J^{+}(q)=\Big\{K=(k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}):n\geq 1,k_{0}\geq 1,k_{1},\ldots,k_{n-1}\geq 0,\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}k_{i}\leq q-1\Big\}.

Then

H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},\infty)/\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})=\mathbb{F}_{2}[N_{K}:K\in J^{+}(q)]

as algebras with multiplicities.
So we have for every
0\leq n\leq\infty
that

H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},m)/\mathfrak{S}_{m})=\mathbb{F}_{2}[N_{K}:K\in J^{+}(q)](m).

In other words
H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},m)/\mathfrak{S}_{m})
has the
\mathbb{F}_{2}
-basis consisting of all monomials in
\mathbb{F}_{2}[N_{K}:K\in J^{+}(q)]
of multiplicities
\leq m
.
This is called Nakamura basis.

(ii)

The homomorphism

i_{*}(F,q)\colon H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},\infty)/\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})\longrightarrow H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},\infty)/\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})

induced by the canonical embeddings
F(\mathbb{R}^{q},m)\subset F(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},m)
,
0\leq m<\infty
, is an injection.
It sends
N_{K}
to the element denoted by the same notation
N_{K}
for
K\in J^{+}(q)
.

Now a part of the commutative diagram (
50
) is considered:

\textstyle{H^{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{n})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(F,q)}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}((F(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{n-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}})^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{*}(F,q)^{2}}
\textstyle{H^{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu^{*}}
\textstyle{\ H^{*}((F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}})^{2}),}

where the maps
i^{*}(F,q)
are induced by the inclusion
\mathbb{R}^{q}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\infty}
.
Observe that

H^{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{n})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})\cong H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})

and

H^{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{n-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}})^{2}\cong H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}}\times\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}}).

It is now claimed that based of
[
64
, Thm. 3.4]
, which we quoted in full, and the definition of the algebra structure on
H_{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},\infty)/\mathfrak{S}_{\infty})
, see
[
63
, Sec. 2]
, the following equality holds:

\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)=\operatorname{span}\{N_{k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}}^{*}:k_{0}\geq 1\},

where
N_{k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}}^{*}
is the dual of the Nakamura element
N_{k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}}
.
Furthermore

\displaystyle\ker(\mu^{*})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{span}\{N_{k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}}^{*}:(k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1})\in J^{+}(q)\}

\displaystyle=i^{*}(F,q)\big(\operatorname{span}\{N_{k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}}^{*}:k_{0}\geq 1\}\big)

\displaystyle=i^{*}(F,q)\big(\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\big).

In particular, the map
i^{*}(F,q)|_{\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)}
is surjective.
With this parts (A) and (B) of
Hu’ng
’s injectivity proof are concluded.

(C)

In
[
64
, Prop. 3.5]
the image
(i^{*}(M,q)\circ i^{*}(\infty,n))\big(\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\big)
was described.
We present this proposition with the paragraph that precedes it as in the original.

On the other, let
\rho_{2^{n}}\colon\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{O}(2^{n})
denote the natural representation of the symmetric group
\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}
in the orthogonal group
\mathrm{O}(2^{n})
. As it is well known:

H^{*}(\mathrm{O}(2^{n}))=\mathbb{Z}_{2}[W_{1},\dots,W_{2^{n}}],

where
W_{i}
denotes the
i
th universal Stiefel--Whitney class (of dimension
i
).
We define
(2^{n}-2^{s})
th Stiefel–Whitney class of
\rho_{2^{n}}
by putting

W_{n,s}=\rho_{2^{n}}^{*}W_{2^{n}-2^{s}},\hskip 18.49988pt0\leq s<n.

Further, we set

\overline{Q}_{n,s}=\mathrm{Res}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n},2},\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})(W_{n,s})\in H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n},2}),\hskip 18.49988pt0\leq s<n.

3.5. Proposition
.
Let

\begin{array}[]{l}i(M,q)\colon M(q,n)\longrightarrow M(\infty,n),\\
i(\infty,n)\colon M(\infty,n)\longrightarrow F(\mathbb{R}^{\infty},2^{n})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}\end{array}

be well-known embeddings.
Then we have

(i^{*}(M,q)\circ i^{*}(\infty,n))\big(\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\big)=\overline{Q}_{n,0}\,\mathbb{F}_{2}[\overline{Q}_{n,0},\ldots,\overline{Q}_{n,n-1}]/I(\overline{Q},q).

Here
I(\overline{Q},q)
denotes the ideal of
\overline{Q}_{n,0}\,\mathbb{F}_{2}[\overline{Q}_{n,0},\ldots,\overline{Q}_{n,n-1}]
generated by monomials of degree
q
.

The proof given by
Hu’ng
proceeds as follows.
According to Lemma
3.14
, the decomposition (
39
), and the fact that
H^{*}(\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}};\mathbb{F}_{2})
is detected by the subgroups
E^{n}
and
\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n-1}}^{2}
we have that

\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)=\langle W_{n,0}\rangle=W_{n,0}\,\mathbb{F}_{2}[W_{n,0},\ldots,W_{n,n-1}].

Consequently, using the notation dictionary
i^{*}(\infty,n)=\operatorname{res}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}_{\mathcal{S}_{2^{n}}}
, we get

\displaystyle i^{*}(\infty,n)\big(\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\big)

\displaystyle=i^{*}(\infty,n)\big(\langle W_{n,0}\rangle\big)

\displaystyle=i^{*}(\infty,n)\big(W_{n,0}\,\mathbb{F}_{2}[W_{n,0},\ldots,W_{n,n-1}]\big)

\displaystyle=\overline{Q}_{n,0}\,\mathbb{F}_{2}[\overline{Q}_{n,0},\ldots,\overline{Q}_{n,n-1}].

On the other hand from Lemma
3.13
we have that

i^{*}(\infty,n)\big(\langle W_{n,0}\rangle\big)\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{2}[\overline{V}_{n,1},\ldots,\overline{V}_{n,n}].

Thus,

i^{*}(\infty,n)\big(\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\big)=\overline{Q}_{n,0}\,\mathbb{F}_{2}[\overline{Q}_{n,0},\ldots,\overline{Q}_{n,n-1}]\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{2}[\overline{V}_{n,1},\ldots,\overline{V}_{n,n}].

Now from Theorem
3.11
we have that

i^{*}(M,q)(\mathbb{F}_{2}[\overline{V}_{n,1},\ldots,\overline{V}_{n,n}])\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[\overline{V}_{n,1},\ldots,\overline{V}_{n,n}]/\langle\overline{V}_{n,1}^{q},\ldots,\overline{V}_{n,n}^{q}\rangle.

Therefore,

(i^{*}(M,q)\circ i^{*}(\infty,n))\big(\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\big)\cong\overline{Q}_{n,0}\,\mathbb{F}_{2}[\overline{Q}_{n,0},\ldots,\overline{Q}_{n,n-1}]/I(\overline{Q},q)

where ideal
I(\overline{Q},q)
is given by

I(\overline{Q},q)=\overline{Q}_{n,0}\,\mathbb{F}_{2}[\overline{Q}_{n,0},\ldots,\overline{Q}_{n,n-1}]\cap\langle\overline{V}_{n,1}^{q},\ldots,\overline{V}_{n,n}^{q}\rangle.

(51)

In order to complete the step (C) in the proof of
[
64
, Prop. 3.5]
it remains to show that the ideal
I(\overline{Q},q)
is the ideal generated by the monomials of degree
q
.
The necessary argument for this was given in
[
64
, Lem. 3.19]
by using the restriction homomorphism
\operatorname{res}^{\mathcal{S}_{2^{n}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{n}}
and considering the corresponding claim in
H^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{n})
.
We give the original formulation without introducing new variables.

3.19. Lemma
.
Let

\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{n,1},\ldots,V_{n,n}]\longrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{n,1},\ldots,V_{n,n}]/\langle V_{n,1}^{q},\ldots,V_{n,n}^{q}\rangle

be the projection.
Then, for the subring
\mathbb{F}_{2}[Q_{n,0},\ldots,Q_{n,n-1}]
of
\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{n,1},\ldots,V_{n,n}]
, we have

\mathrm{pr}\big(\mathbb{F}_{2}[Q_{n,0},\ldots,Q_{n,n-1}]\big)=\mathbb{F}_{2}[Q_{n,0},\ldots,Q_{n,n-1}]/I(Q,q).

Here
I(Q,q)
denotes the ideal of
\mathbb{F}_{2}[Q_{n,0},\ldots,Q_{n,n-1}]
generated by monomials of degree
q
.
II
II

II

The second critical point that is explained in Claim
4.5
.

It will turn out that the ideal
I(Q,q)
is
not
the ideal of
\mathbb{F}_{2}[Q_{n,0},\ldots,Q_{n,n-1}]
generated by monomials of degree
q
, implying that the claim of
[
64
, Lem. 3.19]
does not stand.

First we equivalently transform the statement of the lemma.
Recall that the classes
Q_{n,0},\ldots,Q_{n,n-1}
are Dickson invariants and therefore
\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
-invariants, while

V_{n,1},\ldots,V_{n,n}
are
\mathrm{L}_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
-invariants.
Furthermore, from (
42
) we have that

Q_{n,r}=(\chi_{n}Q_{n-1,r})\,V_{n,n}+(\chi_{n}Q_{n-1,r-1})^{2}

(52)

where
\chi_{n}\in\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
can be interpreted as the variable change in
\mathbb{F}_{2}[y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}]
given by
y_{i}\longmapsto y_{n-i+1}
for
1\leq i\leq n
.
Applying
\chi_{n}
to the equality (
52
) yields

\chi_{n}Q_{n,r}=(\chi_{n}^{2}Q_{n-1,r})\,\chi_{n}V_{n,n}+(\chi_{n}^{2}Q_{n-1,r-1})^{2}.

Since
\chi_{n}^{2}=\operatorname{id}
and
Q_{n,r}
is a
\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{2})
-invariant we get the following recurrent relation

Q_{n,r}=Q_{n-1,r}\,(\chi_{n}V_{n,n})+(Q_{n-1,r-1})^{2}.

For simplicity the following notation was introduced
V_{r}:=\chi_{n}V_{n,r}
for all
1\leq r\leq n
.
Then the projection map

\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{n,1},\ldots,V_{n,n}]\longrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{n,1},\ldots,V_{n,n}]/\langle V_{n,1}^{q},\ldots,V_{n,n}^{q}\rangle

can written by

\mathrm{pr}\colon\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}]\longrightarrow\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{1},\ldots,V_{n}]/\langle V_{1}^{q},\ldots,V_{n}^{q}\rangle.

Thus
[
64
, Lem. 3.19]
, equivalently, states that

\mathrm{pr}\big(\mathbb{F}_{2}[Q_{n,0},\ldots,Q_{n,n-1}]\big)=\mathbb{F}_{2}[Q_{n,0},\ldots,Q_{n,n-1}]/I(Q,q),

where
I(Q,q)
is the ideal of
\mathbb{F}_{2}[Q_{n,0},\ldots,Q_{n,n-1}]
generated by monomials of degree
q
, and

Q_{n,r}=Q_{n-1,r}\,V_{n}+(Q_{n-1,r-1})^{2},

(53)

where we assume that
Q_{k,k}=1
and
Q_{k,-1}=0
for any integer
k\geq 1
.
In particular, from the second equality in (
44
) we get that

Q_{n,0}=V_{1}\cdots V_{n}.

(54)

Now we explain the problem that occurs in the description of the ideal
I(Q,q)
.

Claim 4.5
.

The ideal
I(Q,q)
, defined in (
51
), is not in general the ideal of the ring
\mathbb{F}_{2}[Q_{n,0},\ldots,Q_{n,n-1}]
generated by the monomials of degree
q
.
For example, this fails for
n=2
and
q=3
or
q=4
.

Proof.

The proof is given by exhibiting several counterexamples in the case when
n=2
.
From equalities (
53
) and (
54
) we get that

Q_{2,0}=V_{1}V_{2}\qquad\qquad\text{and}\qquad\qquad Q_{2,1}=V_{2}+V_{1}^{2}.

Now we discuss different values of
q
.

(1)

Let
q=3
.
Then by a direct computation in the ring
\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{1},V_{2}]
we have that monomials

Q_{2,0}^{3}=V_{1}^{3}V_{2}^{3},\quad Q_{2,0}^{2}Q_{2,1}=V_{1}^{2}V_{2}^{3}+V_{1}^{4}V_{2}^{2},\quad Q_{2,0}Q_{2,1}^{2}=V_{1}V_{2}^{3}+V_{1}^{5}V_{2}

belong to the ideal
I(Q,3)
.
Furthermore, since
Q_{2,0}^{2}=V_{1}^{2}V_{2}^{2}\notin I(Q,3)
and

Q_{2,1}^{3}=V_{2}^{3}+V_{2}^{2}V_{1}^{2}+V_{2}V_{1}^{4}+V_{1}^{6}\notin I(Q,3)

we obtain that the monomial

Q_{2,0}^{2}+Q_{2,1}^{3}=V_{2}^{3}+V_{2}V_{1}^{4}+V_{1}^{6}\ \in\ I(Q,3).

Thus,
Q_{2,1}^{3}\notin I(Q,3)
and
Q_{2,0}^{2}+Q_{2,1}^{3}\in I(Q,3)
giving us the first counterexample to the description of the ideal
I(Q,q)
.

(2)

Let
q=4
.
Then working in the ring
\mathbb{F}_{2}[V_{1},V_{2}]
we get that the monomials

\displaystyle Q_{2,0}^{4}=V_{1}^{4}V_{2}^{4},

\displaystyle\qquad Q_{2,0}^{3}Q_{2,1}=V_{1}^{3}V_{2}^{4}+V_{1}^{5}V_{2}^{3},

\displaystyle Q_{2,0}^{2}Q_{2,1}^{2}=V_{1}^{2}V_{2}^{4}+V_{1}^{6}V_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\qquad Q_{2,1}^{4}=V_{1}^{8}+V_{2}^{4}

are in the ideal
I(Q,4)
,while

Q_{2,0}Q_{2,1}^{3}=V_{1}V_{2}^{4}+V_{1}^{3}V_{2}^{3}+V_{1}^{5}V_{2}^{2}+V_{1}^{7}V_{2}\ \notin\ I(Q,4).

Thus we obtained yet another evidence that the description of the ideal
I(Q,q)
is incorrect.

This concludes the proof of the claim and opens a question of correct description of the ideal
I(Q,q)
.
∎

We explained an essential gap in the proof of the step (C): “description of the image
(i^{*}(M,q)\circ i^{*}(\infty,n))\big(\ker\big(\operatorname{res}_{\mathfrak{S}^{2}_{2^{n-1}}}^{\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}}}\big)\big)
.”
This automatically invalidates the proof of the next step (D).
Thus based on two gaps explained in Claim
4.4
and Claim
4.5
we have shown that the proof
of
[
64
, Thm. 3.1]
is incorrect.
Moreover, we do not see how the approach taken by
Hu’ng
can be easily repaired.

Remark 4.6
.

The presented gaps also invalidate results of
[
64
, Sec. 4]
.
In particular, counterexamples given in the proof of Claim
4.5
are also counter examples of the equality
[
64
, (4.7)]
that we copy as in the original:

(4.7)
H^{*}(F(\mathbb{R}^{q},2^{n})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{n}})=i^{*}(F,q)R\oplus i^{*}(F,q)(\langle W_{n,0}\rangle)
.

4.2.
Proof of the Injectivity Theorem

In this section we prove that the map

\rho_{d,2^{m}}^{*}\colon H^{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

is a monomorphism for all
d\geq 2
and all
m\geq 0
.
For the case when
d=\infty
, in Remark
4.3
, we already explained why
\rho_{\infty,2^{m}}^{*}
is a monomorphism.

The proof we present is by induction on
m
.
For
m=0
we have that

\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{0})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{0}}=\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{0})=\{\mathrm{pt}\}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{0})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{0}}=\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{0})=\mathbb{R}^{d},

where
\operatorname{ecy}_{d,2^{0}}\colon\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{0})\longrightarrow\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{0})
is given my
\mathrm{pt}\longmapsto 0\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Thus,
\rho_{d,2^{0}}^{*}
is obviously an isomorphism and consequently a monomorphism.
Let
m\geq 1
, and let us assume that

\rho_{d,2^{m-1}}^{*}\colon H^{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

is a monomorphism.
From Corollary
3.8
we have that the map:

(\rho_{d,2^{m-1}}\times\rho_{d,2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id}\colon\\
\qquad\qquad(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}\longrightarrow\hfill\\
(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}

induces a monomorphism
((\rho_{d,2^{m-1}}\times\rho_{d,2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id})^{*}
in cohomology.
Since from Definition
2.1
we know that

\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}=(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}

we have obtained the monomorphism
((\rho_{d,2^{m-1}}\times\rho_{d,2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id})^{*}
in cohomology:

H^{*}((\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow\\
H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

Next, consider the following diagram of spaces

\textstyle{\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{d,2^{m}}}
\scriptstyle{(\rho_{d,2^{m-1}})^{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\ \operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}
\textstyle{(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})^{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}S^{d-1}}
\textstyle{(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})^{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\operatorname{id})^{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}r}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})^{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\operatorname{ev}_{d,2^{m-1}}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})^{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{ev}_{d,2}}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{d,2^{m}}}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ev}_{d,2^{m}}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}}

(55)

which “commutes up to a homotopy.”
Here

—

r\colon\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2)\longrightarrow S^{d-1}
is the deformation retraction
r(x_{1},x_{2}):=\frac{x_{1}-x_{2}}{\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|}
,

—

\mathcal{C}_{d}
is the little
d
-cubes operad, consult Definition
7.8
,

—

\operatorname{ev}_{d,n}\colon\mathcal{C}_{d}(n)\longrightarrow\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},n)
is the evaluation map introduced in (
100
) which is an
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
-equivariant homotopy equivalence, as stated in Lemma
7.9
, and

—

\mu_{d,2^{m}}
is induced by the structural map of the little
d
-cubes operad:

\mu\colon(\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m-1})\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m-1}))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m}).

(See Definition
7.8
.)

What we mean by “commutes up to a homotopy” here is that the diagram (
55
), after substituting the maps
(\operatorname{id})^{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}r
and
(\operatorname{ev}_{d,2^{m-1}}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})^{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{ev}_{d,2}
with its homotopy inverses, becomes commutative up to a homotopy.
Since we know that the maps
(\operatorname{id})^{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}r
,
(\operatorname{ev}_{d,2^{m-1}}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})^{2}\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{ev}_{d,2}
and
\operatorname{ev}_{d,2^{m}}/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}}
are homotopy equivalences, and
((\rho_{d,2^{m-1}}\times\rho_{d,2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\operatorname{id})^{*}
is an injection by induction hypothesis, we obtained the following claim.

Lemma 4.7
.

If the homomorphism

(\mu_{d,2^{m}})^{*}\colon H^{*}(\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow\\
H^{*}((\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(2);\mathbb{F}_{2})

is a monomorphism, then the homomorphism

\rho_{d,2^{m}}^{*}\colon H^{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H^{*}(\operatorname{Pe}(\mathbb{R}^{d},2^{m})/\mathcal{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

is also a monomorphism.

Hence, the induction step, in the proof of Theorem
4.1
, would follow from the proof of injectivity of the homomorphism
(\mu_{d,2^{m}})^{*}
.
To conclude the induction step, and consequently complete the proof of Theorem
4.1
, we show the following dual theorem.

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
d\geq 2
and
m\geq 1
be integers.
The homomorphism

(\mu_{d,2^{m}})_{*}\colon H_{*}((\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(2);\mathbb{F}_{2})\\
\longrightarrow H_{*}(\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

(56)

is an epimorphism.

For the proof of the theorem we use results of many authors that we first review in generality we need, and
then in Section
4.2.2
we give the proof of Theorem
4.8
.
This will finalize the proof of Theorem
4.1
.

4.2.1.
Prerequisites

Let
X
be a path-connected space in
\operatorname{\mathrm{T}op_{pt}}
.
The free
\mathcal{C}_{d}
-space generated by
X
is defined as the quotient space

\mathcal{C}_{d}(X):=\Big(\coprod_{m\geq 0}\mathcal{C}_{d}(m)\times_{\mathfrak{S}_{m}}X^{m}\Big)/_{\approx},

where for
(\vec{c}_{1},\ldots,\vec{c}_{m})\in\mathcal{C}_{d}(m)
and
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m-1},x_{m})\in X^{m}
, with
x_{m}=\mathrm{pt}
the base point, we define

((\vec{c}_{1},\ldots,\vec{c}_{m-1},\vec{c}_{m}),(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m-1},x_{m}))\approx((\vec{c}_{1},\ldots,\vec{c}_{m-1}),(x_{1},\ldots,x_{m-1})).

(For more details consult
[
76
, Cons. 2.4]
or Section
7.1.5
.)
The space
\mathcal{C}_{d}(X)
is equipped with the natural filtration given by the number of cubes, that is for
k\geq 0
we define

\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X):=\operatorname{im}\Big(\coprod_{0\leq m\leq k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(m)\times_{\mathfrak{S}_{m}}X^{m}\longrightarrow\\
\coprod_{m\geq 0}\mathcal{C}_{d}(m)\times_{\mathfrak{S}_{m}}X^{m}\longrightarrow\Big(\coprod_{m\geq 0}\mathcal{C}_{d}(m)\times_{\mathfrak{S}_{m}}X^{m}\Big)/_{\approx}\Big),

where the first map is the obvious inclusion and the second map is the identification map.
Thus, we obtained the filtration of
\mathcal{C}_{d}(X)
:

\varnothing=\mathrm{F}_{-1}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{F}_{0}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{F}_{1}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X)\subseteq\dots\subseteq\mathrm{F}_{k-1}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X)\subseteq\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X)\subseteq\cdots,

where each pair of spaces
(\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X),\mathrm{F}_{k-1}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X))
is an NDR-pair; see
[
76
, Prop. 2.6]
.
Further on we denote successive quotients by

\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X):=\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X)/\mathrm{F}_{k-1}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X).

Recall that we have set
\mathrm{F}_{-1}\mathcal{C}_{d}(X)=\varnothing
.
Next, we introduce the real
k
-dimensional vector bundle
\xi_{d,k}
over the quotient space
\mathcal{C}_{d}(k)/\mathfrak{S}_{k}
by

\textstyle{\mathbb{R}^{k}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{C}_{d}(k)\times_{\mathfrak{S}_{k}}\mathbb{R}^{k}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{C}_{d}(k)/\mathfrak{S}_{k},}

(57)

where
\mathbb{R}^{k}
is assumed to be the real
\mathfrak{S}_{k}
-representation with the action given by permutation of the coordinates.
The facts we are going to use — in the case when the space
X
is a sphere — are collected in the following theorem, see
[
26
, Thm. A-C]
,
[
39
]
and
[
40
, Thm. 2.6]
.

Theorem 4.9
.

Let
L\geq 1
and
N\geq 1
be integers.

(1)

The space
\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})
is homeomorphic to the Thom space
of the vector bundle
\xi_{d,k}^{\oplus L}
, that is
\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\approx\operatorname{Th}(\xi_{d,k}^{\oplus L})
.
Consequently, for every
i\geq 0
there is the Thom isomorphism

\widetilde{H}_{i+Lk}(\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\widetilde{H}_{i+Lk}(\operatorname{Th}(\xi_{d,k}^{\oplus L}))\cong\\
H_{i}(\mathcal{C}_{d}(k)/\mathfrak{S}_{k};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H_{i}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},k)/\mathfrak{S}_{k};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

(2)

For
N
large enough there is a homotopy equivalence

\Sigma^{N}\big(\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\big)\simeq\Sigma^{N}\big(\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\vee\mathrm{F}_{k-1}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\big).

Here
\Sigma(X)
denotes the suspension of the spaces
X
.

The Approximation theorem of May, Theorem
7.10
, applied to the sphere
S^{L}
, where
L\geq 1
is an integer, yields the weak homotopy equivalence

\alpha_{d}\colon\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\longrightarrow\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L}.

In particulart,
\alpha_{d}
induces the isomorphism in homology with
\mathbb{F}_{2}
coefficients:

(\alpha_{d})_{*}\colon H_{*}(\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

(The results in
[
33
, Sec. 3]
describe further properties of the homology isomorphism
(\alpha_{d})_{*}
.)
The homology of the iterated loop space
\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L}=\Omega^{d}S^{d+L}
with
\mathbb{F}_{2}
coefficients was described as a Pontryagin ring by Araki and Kudo in their seminal paper
[
70
, Thm. 7.1]
using, what we call now, Araki--Kudo--Dyer--Lashof homology operations; see Section
7.1.6
for more details.

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
d\geq 1
and
L\geq 1
be integers.
The homology
H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})
, as a Pontryagin ring, is a polynomial algebra generated by a generator
u_{L}
and all
Q_{i_{1}}Q_{i_{2}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}u_{L}
where
1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq\cdots\leq i_{s}\leq d-1
.
Furthermore,
\deg(u_{L})=L
and

\deg(Q_{i_{1}}Q_{i_{2}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}u_{L})=i_{1}+2i_{2}+4i_{3}+\cdots+2^{s-1}i_{s}+2^{s}L.

We use notation

H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\\
\mathbb{F}_{2}\big[\{u_{L}\}\cup\{Q_{i_{1}}Q_{i_{2}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}u_{L}\,:\,s\geq 1,\,1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq\cdots\leq i_{s}\leq d-1\}\big].

Remark 4.11
.

The generators of the homology
H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})
have a concrete geometric description.
Consider the map
e_{d}\colon S^{L}\longrightarrow\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L}
that corresponds to the identity map
\operatorname{id}\colon\Sigma^{d}S^{L}\longrightarrow\Sigma^{d}S^{L}
along the adjunction
[A,\Omega^{d}B]_{\mathrm{pt}}\longleftrightarrow[\Sigma^{d}A,B]_{\mathrm{pt}}
.
Here
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathrm{pt}}
denotes the set of all homotopy classes of pointed maps between the pointed spaces.
After taking
d
-fold suspension, or the smash product with the sphere
S^{d}
, we get the following commutative triangle

\textstyle{\Sigma^{d}S^{L}=S^{d}\wedge S^{L}\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\wedge e_{d}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\ \Sigma^{d}\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L}=S^{d}\wedge(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{ev_{d}}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{d}S^{L}=S^{d}\wedge S^{L},}

where
ev_{d}\colon\Sigma^{d}\Omega^{d}X\longrightarrow X
is the evaluation map.
Thus, the induced map in homology

(e_{d})_{*}\colon H_{*}(S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})

is a monomorphism.
The generator
u_{L}
, the so called fundamental class of
\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L}
, is the image of the generator of
H_{L}(S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})
along
(e_{d})_{*}
.
Now the generators
Q_{i_{1}}Q_{i_{2}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}u_{L}
for
1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq\cdots\leq i_{s}\leq d-1
are images of
u_{L}
under the sequence
Q_{i_{1}}Q_{i_{2}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}
of Araki--Kudo--Dyer--Lashof homology operations.
Thus we have complete description of the homology
H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})
.

Next we define a filtration of the polynomial algebra

\mathbb{F}_{2}\big[\{u_{L}\}\cup\{Q_{i_{1}}Q_{i_{2}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}u_{L}\,:\,s\geq 1,\,1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq\cdots\leq i_{s}\leq d-1\}\big]\cong\\
H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})

by defining the weight function
\omega
on its monomials as follows:

\omega(u_{L})=1,\qquad\qquad\omega(Q_{i}u)=2\omega(u),\qquad\qquad\omega(v_{1}\cdot v_{2})=\omega(v_{1})+\omega(v_{2}),

where
u
is an algebra generator, and
v_{1},v_{2}
are monomials is algebra generators.
In particular, the weight of algebra generators are alway powers of two, that is

\omega(Q_{i_{1}}Q_{i_{2}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}u_{L})=2\omega(Q_{i_{2}}Q_{i_{3}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}u_{L})=\cdots=2^{s}.

Now, for
k\geq 0
, we set
\mathcal{F}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})
to be the vector subspace of the polynomial ring
H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})
generated by all monomials with weight at most
k
.
In this way we obtained a filtration of the polynomial ring
H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})
by vector spaces:

0=\mathcal{F}_{0}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{1}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\subseteq\\
\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{k-1}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\subseteq\cdots,

the so called
weight filtration
.
Furthermore we denote the sequence of quotients by

\mathcal{D}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2}):=\mathcal{F}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})/\mathcal{F}_{k-1}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

Example 4.12
.

In order to illustrate the notion of the weight filtration in the following table we list all the generators of
H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})
with weight at most
4
.

Thus, for example:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle=\text{the }\mathbb{F}_{2}\text{-vector space with a basis }\{u_{L}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{2}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle=\text{the }\mathbb{F}_{2}\text{-vector space with a basis }\{u_{L},u_{L}^{2},Q_{i}u_{L}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{3}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle=\text{the }\mathbb{F}_{2}\text{-vector space with a basis }

\displaystyle\quad\ \{u_{L},u_{L}^{2},Q_{i}u_{L},u_{L}^{3},u_{L}Q_{i}u_{L}\}.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle=\text{the }\mathbb{F}_{2}\text{-vector space with a basis }\{u_{L}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle=\text{the }\mathbb{F}_{2}\text{-vector space with a basis }\{u_{L}^{2},Q_{i}u_{L}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{3}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle=\text{the }\mathbb{F}_{2}\text{-vector space with a basis }\{u_{L}^{3},u_{L}Q_{i}u_{L}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{4}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})

\displaystyle=\text{the }\mathbb{F}_{2}\text{-vector space with a basis }

\displaystyle\quad\ \{u_{L}^{4},u_{L}^{2}Q_{i}u_{L},Q_{i}u_{L}Q_{j}u_{L},Q_{i}Q_{j}u_{L}\}.

For more details calculation of this type see
[
26
, Sec. 5]
.

The central property of the filtration we just defined is given in the following theorem, see
[
33
, Cor. 3.3]
.

Theorem 4.13
.

Let
d\geq 2
and
L\geq 1
be integers.
For every
k\geq 0
there is an isomorphism of graded vector spaces

\alpha_{d,k}\colon H_{*}(\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

Moreover, for every
k\geq 0
there is an isomorphism of vector spaces

\overline{\alpha}_{d,k}\colon\widetilde{H}_{*}(\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2}),

such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{H_{*}(\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{d,k}}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{F}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{H}_{*}(\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\alpha}_{d,k}}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{D}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2}).}

Here the left vertical map is induced by the quotient map of topological spaces

\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\longrightarrow\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})=\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})/\mathrm{F}_{k-1}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}),

while the right vertical map is the quotient map of vector spaces

\mathcal{F}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\\
=\mathcal{F}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})/\mathcal{F}_{k-1}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

The results presented in
[
33
, Sec. 3 and Sec.4]
imply that the isomorphisms
\alpha_{d,k}
and
\overline{\alpha}_{d,k}
are induced by the isomorphism
(\alpha_{d})_{*}
.

Now, directly from Theorem
4.9
and Theorem
4.13
, we get a description of homology of the unordered configuration space with
\mathbb{F}_{2}
coefficients, see
[
26
, Sec. 4.4]
.

Corollary 4.14
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
k\geq 1
and
L\geq 1
be integers.
There is an isomorphism of graded vector space

H_{*-kL}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},k)/\mathfrak{S}_{k};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\mathcal{D}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2}).

Alternatively, we can describe the homology of the unordered configurations space
\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},k)/\mathfrak{S}_{k}
with
\mathbb{F}_{2}
coefficients in the following way.

Corollary 4.15
.

Let
d\geq 2
,
k\geq 1
,
L\geq 1
and
0\leq i\leq(d-1)(k-1)
be integers.
The homology of the unordered configurations space

H_{i}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},k)/\mathfrak{S}_{k};\mathbb{F}_{2})

is isomorphic to the
\mathbb{F}_{2}
vector space spanned by all monomials of degree
i+kL
of the polynomial ring

\mathbb{F}_{2}\big[\{u_{L}\}\cup\{Q_{i_{1}}Q_{i_{2}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}u_{L}\,:\,s\geq 1,\,1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq\cdots\leq i_{s}\leq d-1\}\big]

whose weights are exactly
k
.

In order to illustrate the previous result we give a few simple examples of evaluation of the homology of the unordered configuration space.

Example 4.16
.

From Corollary
4.15
we have that the homology
H_{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{d},k)/\mathfrak{S}_{k};\mathbb{F}_{2})
of the unordered configuration space is

(1)

for the case
d=2
and
k=2
spanned in

dimension
0
by the monomial:

u_{L}^{2}
,

dimension
1
by the monomial:

Q_{1}u_{L}
,

(Thus, indeed
H_{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{2},2)/\mathfrak{S}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H_{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1};\mathbb{F}_{2})
as expected.)

(2)

for the case
d=2
and
k=3
spanned in

dimension
0
by the monomial:

u_{L}^{3}
,

dimension
1
by the monomial:

u_{L}^{3}\,(Q_{1}u_{L})
,

(3)

for the case
d=2
and
k=4
spanned in

dimension
0
by the monomial:

u_{L}^{4}
,

dimension
1
by the monomial:

u_{L}^{2}\,(Q_{1}u_{L})
,

dimension
2
by the monomial:

(Q_{1}u_{L})\,(Q_{1}u_{L})
,

dimension
3
by the monomial:

Q_{1}Q_{1}u_{L}
,

(4)

for the case
d=3
and
k=2
spanned in

dimension
0
by the monomial:

u_{L}^{2}
,

dimension
1
by the monomial:

Q_{1}u_{L}
,

dimension
1
by the monomial:

Q_{2}u_{L}
,

(Again, we see that
H_{*}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{3},2)/\mathfrak{S}_{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H_{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{2};\mathbb{F}_{2})
as expected.)

(5)

for the case
d=3
and
k=4
spanned in

dimension
0
by the monomial:

u_{L}^{4}
,

dimension
1
by the monomial:

u_{L}^{2}\,(Q_{1}u_{L})
,

dimension
2
by the monomials:

u_{L}^{2}\,(Q_{2}u_{L})
and
(Q_{1}u_{L})\,(Q_{1}u_{L})
,

dimension
3
by the monomials:

(Q_{1}u_{L})\,(Q_{2}u_{L})
and
Q_{1}Q_{1}u_{L}
,

dimension
4
by the monomial:

(Q_{2}u_{L})\,(Q_{2}u_{L})
,

dimension
5
by the monomial:

Q_{1}Q_{2}u_{L}
,

dimension
6
by the monomial:

Q_{2}Q_{2}u_{L}
,

Thus, we have that

H_{i}(\operatorname{F}(\mathbb{R}^{3},4)/\mathfrak{S}_{4};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\begin{cases}\mathbb{F}_{2},&i=0,1,4,5,6,\\
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{\oplus 2},&i=3,4,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

With this result we collected all necessary ingredients that we need for the proof of Theorem
4.8
.

4.2.2.
Proof of the dual Epimorphism Theorem

Let the integers
d\geq 2
and
m\geq 1
be fixed.
Now we prove that the following homomorphism, induced by a structural map of the little cubes operad
\mathcal{C}_{d}
,

(\mu_{d,2^{m}})_{*}\colon H_{*}((\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}}\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m-1})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m-1}})\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(2);\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow\\
H_{*}(\mathcal{C}_{d}(2^{m})/\mathfrak{S}_{2^{m}};\mathbb{F}_{2})

is an epimorphism.

Let
L\geq 1
be an arbitrary integer, and consider the free
\mathcal{C}_{d}
-space
\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})
.
From Theorem
4.10
, using the the weak homotopy equivalence

\alpha_{d}\colon\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\longrightarrow\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L},

we get the following isomorphisms

H_{*}(\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}\mathcal{D}_{k}H_{*}(\Omega^{d}\Sigma^{d}S^{L};\mathbb{F}_{2})\cong\\
\mathbb{F}_{2}\big[\{u_{L}\}\cup\{Q_{i_{1}}Q_{i_{2}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}u_{L}\,:\,s\geq 1,\,1\leq i_{1}\leq i_{2}\leq\cdots\leq i_{s}\leq d-1\}\big].

Since
\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})
is a
\mathcal{C}_{d}
-space, as explained in Lemma
7.6
, there exists a map

\textstyle{\Theta_{2}\colon(\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}).}

(In the following we will abuse notation and all maps induced by
\Theta_{2}
will be denoted in the same way.)
On the level of space filtration, for integers
k_{1}\geq 0
and
k_{2}\geq 0
, we have that
\Theta_{2}
induces a map

\textstyle{\Theta_{2}\colon(\mathrm{F}_{k_{1}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathrm{F}_{k_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{F}_{k_{1}+k_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}).}

Thus, for any integer
k\geq 0
we have an induced map on quotient spaces

\textstyle{\Theta_{2}\colon(\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{D}_{2k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}).}

The symmetric group
\mathfrak{S}_{2}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
acts naturally on: the space
\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)
of pairs of little
d
-cubes by interchanging the cubes, and on the product
\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})
by interchanging the factors.
If the trivial action on the space
\mathrm{D}_{2k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})
is assumed, the equivariance property of the structural map of the little cubes operad implies that the last
\Theta_{2}
map is an
\mathfrak{S}_{2}
-equivariant map.
Consequently, it induces maps such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{(\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta_{2}}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{\Theta_{2}}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{F}_{2k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{2}(2)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{\Theta_{2}}
\textstyle{\ \mathrm{D}_{2k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}).}

Here the vertical maps are either inclusions or quotient maps.
Passing to homology we get the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{H_{*}((\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(2);\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(\Theta_{2})_{*}}
\textstyle{\ H_{*}(\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})}
\textstyle{H_{*}((\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{d}(2);\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(\Theta_{2})_{*}}
\textstyle{\ H_{*}(\mathrm{F}_{2k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{*}((\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{2}(2);\mathbb{F}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(\Theta_{2})_{*}}
\textstyle{\ H_{*}(\mathrm{D}_{2k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2}).}

(58)

In the case when
k=2^{m-1}
the homomorphism

(\Theta_{2})_{*}\colon H_{*}((\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{2}(2);\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow\\
H_{*}(\mathrm{D}_{2k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})

(59)

coincides with the map
(\mu_{d,2^{m}})_{*}
from (
56
) — after appropriate dimension shift by
-2kL
.
Therefore in order to complete the proof of Theorem
4.8
it suffices to prove that the homomorphism
(\Theta_{2})_{*}
, from (
59
), is surjective.

The map
H_{*}(\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow H_{*}(\mathrm{D}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})
is surjective for every
k
.
Therefore, using Corollary
3.9
and commutativity of the diagram (
58
), we should first consider the homomorphism

(\Theta_{2})_{*}\colon H_{*}((\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L})\times\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L}))\times_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\mathcal{C}_{2}(2);\mathbb{F}_{2})\longrightarrow\\
H_{*}(\mathrm{F}_{2k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2}).

(60)

For
d\geq 2
, let

\mathcal{I}_{d}:=\{(i_{1},\dots,i_{s}):s\geq 1,\,1\leq i_{1}\leq\cdots\leq i_{s}\leq d-1\}

be the set of all admissible iterations.
Then, every element
I\in\mathcal{I}_{d}
defines the iterated Araki--Kudo--Dyer--Lashof homology operation

Q_{I}:=Q_{i_{1}}Q_{i_{2}}\cdots Q_{i_{s}}.

Hence, as we have seen in Theorem
4.13
, the homology
H_{*}(\mathrm{F}_{k}\mathcal{C}_{d}(S^{L});\mathbb{F}_{2})
can be identified with the vector space spanned by all monomials in, variables from the set
\{Q_{I}u_{L}:I\in\mathcal{I}_{d}\}\cup\{u_{L}\}
with weight at most
k
.
Furthermore, from definition of Araki--Kudo--Dyer--Lashof homology operations given in Section
7.1.6
, it follows that for every
Q_{I}u_{L}
,
I\in\mathcal{I}_{d}
, of weight exactly
2^{m}
, there exists
