\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hom_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}(C_{0}(\widetilde{X});\mathcal{L})\ }
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces 0\ .\qquad\qquad\qquad}

This means that

H^{n}(X;\mathcal{M}):=\frac{\ker\big(\hom_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}(C_{n}(\widetilde{X});\mathcal{L}))\longrightarrow\hom_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}(C_{n+1}(\widetilde{X});\mathcal{L})\big)}{\operatorname{im}\big(\hom_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}(C_{n-1}(\widetilde{X});\mathcal{L})\longrightarrow\hom_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}(C_{n}(\widetilde{X});\mathcal{L})\big)}\ .

For
d=2
the universal cover of the projective space
\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1}
is the real line
\mathbb{R}^{1}
, and the fundamental group
\pi=\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1})=\langle t\rangle
is the infinite cyclic group.
An associated
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-CW-complex of the universal cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1}}=\mathbb{R}^{1}
is defined as follows: The
0
-cells are all integers
\{x_{0}^{i}:=\{i\}:i\in\mathbb{Z}\}
, while the
1
-cells are intervals
\{x_{1}^{i}:=[i,i+1]:i\in\mathbb{Z}\}
.
The action of the generator
t
of the fundamental group on the cells of the
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-CW-complex is given by
t\cdot x_{j}^{i}=x_{j}^{i+1}
for
j\in\{0,1\}
.
Hence, the induced cellular chain complex of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-modules is given by

C_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1}})=\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]=_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]\text{-module}}\langle x_{0}^{1}\rangle\qquad\text{and}\qquad C_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1}})=\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]=_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]\text{-module}}\langle x_{1}^{1}\rangle

where the only non-trivial boundary homomorphism
d_{1}
is defined on the generator of
C_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1}})
by
d_{1}(x_{1}^{1}):=x_{0}^{1}-x_{0}^{0}=(t-1)\cdot x_{0}^{0}
.
Thus, the cellular chain complex we consider in this case is

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ 0.}

After applying the functor
\hom_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]}(\cdot,\mathcal{M})
we get the cochain complex

\textstyle{0\ }
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\ }
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ 0.}

If we recall that additively
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{F}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}
by direct inspection we see that

H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1};\mathcal{M})\cong\begin{cases}\ker\big(\mathcal{M}\overset{(t-1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}\big)\cong\mathbb{F}_{2},&r=0,\\
\mathcal{M}/\operatorname{im}\big(\mathcal{M}\overset{(t-1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}\big)\cong\mathbb{F}_{2},&r=1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

For further use we denote the generator of the group
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{M})
by
z_{1}
.

Now, let
d\geq 3
be an integer.
Then the universal cover of the projective space
\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}
is the sphere
S^{d-1}
, and the fundamental group is
\pi:=\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\langle t\rangle
.
We associate an
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-CW-complex to the universal cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}}=S^{d-1}
as follows:
In each dimension
i
, where
0\leq i\leq d-1
, there are two cells
x_{i}^{0}
and
x_{i}^{1}
.
The generator
t
of the fundamental group
\pi
acts on the cells by
t\cdot x_{j}^{0}=x_{j}^{1}
and
t\cdot x_{j}^{1}=x_{j}^{0}
for
0\leq j\leq d-1
.
Thus, the induced cellular chain complex of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-modules is given by

C_{n}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}})=\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]=_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\text{-module}}\langle x_{n}^{0}\rangle,\qquad\qquad\text{for }0\leq n\leq d-1,

where the boundary homomorphism
d_{n}\colon C_{n}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}})\longrightarrow C_{n-1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}})
on the generator
x_{n}^{0}
is

\displaystyle d_{n}(x_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle:=x_{n-1}^{1}-x_{n-1}^{0}=(t-1)\cdot x_{n-1}^{0},\qquad\qquad\text{for }1\leq n\leq d-1\text{ odd},

\displaystyle d_{n}(x_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle:=x_{n-1}^{1}+x_{n-1}^{0}=(t+1)\cdot x_{n-1}^{0},\qquad\qquad\text{for }1\leq n\leq d-1\text{ even},

and otherwise zero.
The cellular chain complex we obtained in this case is

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ 0,}

when
d
is odd, and

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ 0,}

when
d
is even.

Applying the functor
\hom_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]}(\cdot,\mathcal{M})
we get the cochain complex which is isomorphic to the following cochain complex

\textstyle{0\ }
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\ }
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\cdots\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces 0,}

when
d
is odd, and

\textstyle{0\ }
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\ }
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\cdots\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces 0,}

when
d
is even.
Since
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{F}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}
, and the homomorphisms
\mathcal{M}\overset{(t-1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}\overset{(t+1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}
coincide on
\mathcal{M}
, we conclude that

H^{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{M})\cong\begin{cases}\ker\big(\mathcal{M}\overset{(t-1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}\big)\cong\mathbb{F}_{2},&r=0,\\
\mathcal{M}/\operatorname{im}\big(\mathcal{M}\overset{(t-1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}\big)\cong\mathbb{F}_{2},&r=d-1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We denote the generator of the group
H^{d-1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{M})
by
z_{d-1}
.

7.4.5.
Homology of the real projective space with local coefficients

For
d\geq 2
, along the lines of the previous section, we compute the homology of the projective space

H_{*}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{M})

where the local coefficient system
\mathcal{M}
is additively
\mathbb{F}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}
, and the action of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})=\langle t\rangle
on
\mathcal{M}
is given by
t\cdot(a_{1},a_{2})=(a_{2},a_{1})
where
(a_{1},a_{2})\in\mathbb{F}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}
.

Like in the case of cohomology we consider two separate cases:
d=2
when
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\cong\mathbb{Z}
, and
d\geq 3
when
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.
First we recall the definition of the homology with local coefficients, consult for example
[
60
, Sec. 3.H]
.

For a path-connected CW-complex
X
let
\widetilde{X}
denote its universal cover, and let
\pi:=\pi_{1}(X)
be its fundamental group.
Denote by
\mathcal{L}
a local coefficient system on
X
, that is a
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-module.
Assume that we are given a structure of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-CW-complex on
\widetilde{X}
with associated cellular chain complex

\textstyle{\cdots\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ C_{n+1}(\widetilde{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{d_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\ C_{n}(\widetilde{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{d_{n}}
\textstyle{\ C_{n-1}(\widetilde{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{d_{n-1}}
\textstyle{\ \cdots}
\textstyle{\cdots\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{d_{2}}
\textstyle{\ C_{1}(\widetilde{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{d_{1}}
\textstyle{\ C_{0}(\widetilde{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ 0\ ,}

where
C_{n}(\widetilde{X})
is a
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-module, and
d_{n}\colon C_{n}(\widetilde{X})\longrightarrow C_{n-1}(\widetilde{X})
is a
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-module homomorphism, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

The homology
H_{*}(X;\mathcal{L})
of
X
with local coefficients in
\mathcal{L}
is homology of the chain complex

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ C_{n+1}(\widetilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}\mathcal{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ C_{n}(\widetilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}\mathcal{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ C_{n-1}(\widetilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}\mathcal{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ \cdots}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ C_{1}(\widetilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}\mathcal{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ C_{0}(\widetilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}\mathcal{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ 0\ .\qquad\qquad}

This means that

H_{n}(X;\mathcal{M}):=\frac{\ker\big(C_{n}(\widetilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}\mathcal{L}\longrightarrow C_{n-1}(\widetilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}\mathcal{L}\big)}{\operatorname{im}\big(C_{n+1}(\widetilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}\mathcal{L}\longrightarrow C_{n}(\widetilde{X})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\pi]}\mathcal{L}\big)}\ .

First we consider the case
d=2
.
The universal cover of the projective space
\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1}
is the real line
\mathbb{R}^{1}
, and the fundamental group
\pi=\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1})=\langle t\rangle
is the infinite cyclic group.
An associated
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-CW-complex of the universal cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1}}=\mathbb{R}^{1}
is defined as follows: The
0
-cells are all integers
\{x_{0}^{i}:=\{i\}:i\in\mathbb{Z}\}
, while the
1
-cells are intervals
\{x_{1}^{i}:=[i,i+1]:i\in\mathbb{Z}\}
.
The action of the generator
t
of the fundamental group on the cells of the
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-CW-complex is given by
t\cdot x_{j}^{i}=x_{j}^{i+1}
for
j\in\{0,1\}
.
Hence, the induced cellular chain complex of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-modules is given by

C_{0}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1}})=\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]=_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]\text{-module}}\langle x_{0}^{1}\rangle\qquad\text{and}\qquad C_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1}})=\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]=_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]\text{-module}}\langle x_{1}^{1}\rangle

where the only non-trivial boundary homomorphism
d_{1}
is defined on the generator of
C_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1}})
by
d_{1}(x_{1}^{1}):=x_{0}^{1}-x_{0}^{0}=(t-1)\cdot x_{0}^{0}
.
Thus, the cellular chain complex we consider in this case is

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ 0.}

After applying the functor
\cdot\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]}\mathcal{M}
we get the chain complex

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ 0.}

Since additively
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{F}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}
by direct inspection we see that

H_{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{1};\mathcal{M})\cong\begin{cases}\mathcal{M}/\operatorname{im}\big(\mathcal{M}\overset{(t-1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}\big)\cong\mathbb{F}_{2},&r=0,\\
\ker\big(\mathcal{M}\overset{(t-1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}\big)\cong\mathbb{F}_{2},&r=1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

For further use we denote the generator of the group
H_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{M})
by
h_{1}
.

When
d\geq 3
the universal cover of the projective space
\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}
is the sphere
S^{d-1}
, and the fundamental group is
\pi:=\pi_{1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1})\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}=\langle t\rangle
.
An
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-CW-complex is associated to the universal cover
\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}}=S^{d-1}
as follows:
In each dimension
i
, where
0\leq i\leq d-1
, there are two cells
x_{i}^{0}
and
x_{i}^{1}
.
The generator
t
of the fundamental group
\pi
acts on the cells by
t\cdot x_{j}^{0}=x_{j}^{1}
and
t\cdot x_{j}^{1}=x_{j}^{0}
for
0\leq j\leq d-1
.
Thus, the induced cellular chain complex of
\mathbb{Z}[\pi]
-modules is given by

C_{n}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}})=\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]=_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\text{-module}}\langle x_{n}^{0}\rangle,\qquad\qquad\text{for }0\leq n\leq d-1,

where the boundary homomorphism
d_{n}\colon C_{n}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}})\longrightarrow C_{n-1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1}})
on the generator
x_{n}^{0}
is

\displaystyle d_{n}(x_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle:=x_{n-1}^{1}-x_{n-1}^{0}=(t-1)\cdot x_{n-1}^{0},\qquad\qquad\text{for }1\leq n\leq d-1\text{ odd},

\displaystyle d_{n}(x_{n}^{0})

\displaystyle:=x_{n-1}^{1}+x_{n-1}^{0}=(t+1)\cdot x_{n-1}^{0},\qquad\qquad\text{for }1\leq n\leq d-1\text{ even},

and otherwise zero.
The cellular chain complex we obtained in this case is

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ 0,}

when
d
is odd, and

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ 0,}

when
d
is even.

Applying the functor
\cdot\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{Z}]}\mathcal{M}
yields a chain complex isomorphic to the chain complex:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ 0,}

when
d
is odd, and

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t+1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\scriptstyle{(t-1)\cdot}
\textstyle{\ \mathcal{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ }
\textstyle{\ 0,}

when
d
is even.
Since
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{F}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}
, and the homomorphisms
\mathcal{M}\overset{(t-1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}\overset{(t+1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}
coincide on
\mathcal{M}
, we have that

H_{r}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{M})\cong\begin{cases}\mathcal{M}/\operatorname{im}\big(\mathcal{M}\overset{(t-1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}\big)\cong\mathbb{F}_{2},&r=0,\\
\ker\big(\mathcal{M}\overset{(t-1)\cdot}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{M}\big)\cong\mathbb{F}_{2},&r=d-1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We denote the generator of the group
H_{d-1}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{M})
by
h_{d-1}
, and the generator of the group
H_{0}(\mathbb{R}{\rm P}^{d-1};\mathcal{M})
by
h_{0}
.
